998. **For veto:** the press that focuses the window is told by the surface's own `Focus` bookkeeping (core sends the press before the queued `Focus(true)`), so a click into a board from another DIE window leaves no dot, and its drags and release draw nothing; a click into DIE from another app arrives after the app's `Focus(true)` and draws, since macOS activates the app first; `u` takes the dot back. A `Focus(false)` during a gesture drops the gesture.
999. Undo over an operation log, one entry per gesture, command or label session, each holding the elements before and after with the index each had on the board as it was (`undo::original_index`, since removals one after another shift the rest), so undo puts them back in order whatever order they went; a delete or erase also carries the lines bound to what went, so undo restores their bindings. Ids are never reused; the log keeps 1,000 entries; it's session state, not the file's.
1000. **For veto:** the eraser tests the segment between pointer samples every 2 board px, since a fast drag's samples jump a stroke (the pointer arrives per cell until the drag fix lands); `1`..`6` and `[` `]` restyle the selection too, as one entry, as well as setting what's drawn next; `y` duplicates a cell down and right, keeping a binding between two copied elements and dropping one to an element left behind; a line binds within 12 px of a rectangle or ellipse and is put on its outline (an ellipse's by angle from its centre).
1001. The file, in the dialect the brief settles: plain SVG 1.1 with a `<style>` naming the inks, a `<marker>` per ink, a background rect, and `data-wb-version="1"`, `data-wb-next-id`, `data-wb-cell` on the root; per element `data-wb-id`, `data-wb-ink`, `data-wb-width`, a rectangle's or ellipse's corners as given (`data-wb-a`, `data-wb-b`, since `x y width height` and `cx cy rx ry` would round), a label's text as typed (`data-wb-text`, with `<tspan>`s for browsers), `data-wb-arrow`, `data-wb-from`, `data-wb-to`, `data-wb-hl`. Floats are written as Rust's shortest round-trip form. The reader is a tokenizer for this dialect (tags, attributes, text, entities), skips elements without `data-wb-id`, refuses a root without `data-wb-version` ("not a whiteboard file; open it in a browser") and reads an empty or missing file as a new board. Autosave is one `after` timer, set at the first change and re-armed from the tick until every board has been quiet for a second, written at the session's hand-over and when a buffer is killed, through a temporary file renamed over the old one; the timer's `Sub` is dropped when nothing is dirty, so none runs idle. A failed write shows `NAME: why` in the status line and the whole path in the messages.
1002. The session: `Kept.state` is `{"boards": [{file, pan, zoom, tool, ink, width, selection, shown}]}`, never keyed by the buffer (core writes `Kept.buffers` entries only for buffers it saves itself); `restore_session` reopens each file from disk (the hand-over wrote it), puts the view, tool, ink, width and selection back (a label session comes back as the pen), shows the one used last in the current window as a stand-in for its own until M1's surface restorer, and opens the rest in the background. Restored twice in a row in `tests/session.rs`.
1003. Export and the pasteboard: `whiteboard-export png` rasterizes the model with tiny-skia in a `ctx.spawn` job, labels through fontdue over IoskeleyMonoTerm from the font directory (the option, else `$DIE_FONT_DIR`, else the bundle's `Contents/Resources/fonts`, else the workspace's `fonts/`, die-render's rule repeated since D14 keeps it out of reach), at `png_scale` (2) on `export_background` (`theme`, `white`, `transparent`), to `NAME.png` beside the file; `svg` writes `NAME.export.svg` without the `data-wb-*` attributes. `whiteboard-copy-image` writes a temporary PNG and runs `osascript -e 'set the clipboard to (read (POSIX file "…") as «class PNGf»)'`, or `wl-copy --type image/png` or `xclip -selection clipboard -t image/png`, whichever `die_core::process::path()` has, then removes the file; `whiteboard-open` runs `open` or `xdg-open`. The command lines are tested as built; no test touches the real pasteboard. Core's `Clipboard` stays text-only and `ctx.process` stays M1's.
1004. Gaps met from the public API, for M1's list: a surface without text inherits none of die-vim's text-kind keys, so the kind map names `vim-ex`, the `C-w` table (`vim-window-*`), `vim-cancel` and core's `toggle-max-width` itself, and C8 refuses startup without die-vim registered (`die::built_in()` always has it; die-orchid-client's `vim-insert-newline-bare` was the precedent); there's no theme-change hook, so the inks are resolved again on each event and command (six face lookups); `Ctx::pixels_supported()` landed with M3a's core half (call 992) as this was built, and the surface will read it once pixels.rs exists; until then `pixels::new()` gives none and the cells draw; a surface can't visit a file, so `:w` isn't there and isn't needed; a board's window across a restart waits for M1.
1005. Tests, all headless with `set_cell_pixels` and pointer events with `px`: `tests/tools.rs` (the pen's smoothing, simplification and end point; each shape, Shift, one shot; bindings following a nudge and surviving undo; the eraser crossing and the right button; select's click, move, marquee, nudges, duplicate, delete, reverse video, undo per gesture; labels in insert state with `S-RET`, `BS`, the caret, a double click, `RET`, an empty label, `Cmd-Z`; the focusing press; the wheel and the zoom message; tools by name), `tests/file.rs` (the round trip exact through `svg` and through a second DIE, the export without the dialect, a foreign file refused, `.svg` added, autosave's timing on the fake clock and no timer when clean, a failed write, a kill writing, the prompt descending, `<S-Tab>`, `<M-CR>`, recent first, `:whiteboard`), `tests/export.rs` (the PNG decoded with the png crate: size, the ink's pixel, the theme's background; the options; a label's glyph pixels; the SVG export), `tests/keys.rs` (every binding found by `describe-key` in `kind whiteboard`, insert's keys, `:` and `C-w` composing with die-vim, `\z` with your text defaults, `full_width`), `tests/render.rs` (a small board's cells as text), `tests/session.rs`, `tests/perf.rs`, and `apps/die-dev/tests/whiteboard.rs` (`\w` through your config, `\hk r`, mono-dark's inks). Unit tests in each module.
1006. The numbers, from `tests/perf.rs` on a board of 1,000 strokes of 20 points (580 KB of SVG), test profile (opt-level 1), on this machine while it was otherwise quiet: open 11.5 ms, a key 0.3 ms, a pointer sample during a stroke 0.1 ms, a full cell render 1.5 ms. The budgets it's held to are 500, 200, 100 and 200 ms.
1007. Open: not tried in your DIE.app, since nothing may go on your screen; it's on m0/integration only. The cell stage draws at 1:1 and `+` `-` say so; `0` takes the pan back. Braille ink is coarse and one colour per cell, as the brief accepts. Left out as the brief lists: the vim-like grammar over shapes (slate's design), pressure and tilt, pinch, a pointer cursor shape, `Clipboard::write_image`, a file-type mode for `.svg` through `\f`, images, source rectangles, Hide placements, the display link. Two windows on one board share its view and its frame (C10). A label's wide characters take one cell each on screen. The gate's one failure is xtask's app test `the_restart_loop_finds_equal_digests_and_logs_each_time`, a restart held to 1 s that took 988-1,028 ms here while other packages built on this machine (load 9-15), and 971-1,011 ms at the integration tip (20b7aca) run back to back with it: the machine's, not this package's; the rest of the gate passes, and `cargo xtask cross-check` passes for both Linux targets.

**M3a: placements and pixel buffers, the render half**

1008. The package: the whiteboard's render half (the build brief's Part B, die-render's side) on 20b7aca's API: the placement quad, S1's staging ring as the host's sink, the pending flag, a drag per pointer sample, the `pixels` check and the budget's eighth macOS raise. Nothing of the whiteboard itself; die-term's kitty graphics draw through the same quad when they come. Now something draws: 993's first sentence is closed.
1009. `kind::PIXELS` quads: `rows::Frames::build` emits one per placement from `Scene.placements`, after the backgrounds for z < 0 and after the glyphs otherwise, cursors last, made fresh every frame since the list is tiny and a buffer changes with no cell changing, and `rows::Frame::placed` says each quad's index and buffer. The backend draws the instances in runs (`drawPrimitives…baseInstance`), binding the placement's texture at index 2 for its one quad, with the color atlas standing in before that so the binding the shader names is never empty. The sampler is nearest with `clamp_to_zero`, so a buffer smaller than its cells leaves the rest as the cells drew it and a larger one shows its top-left, 982's rule with no code of its own.
1010. **For veto:** the sink is `platform::macos::pixbuf::Pixels`, made in `window::run` on the system's Metal device (the same device the pipeline opens its own handle to) and connected to core right after the waker, before the window exists, so `pixels_supported()` is true from the first turn and a board restored at startup can choose pixels. The renderer learns it once it exists (`Renderer::show_pixels`); `Renderer::new` keeps its signature, which die-dev's `looks.rs` calls.
1011. The ring, as S1 had it: `RING` = 3 `StorageModeShared` MTLBuffers per buffer, sized to its largest size so far and re-made only when a resize outgrows them, with a slot the GPU is reading kept out until it's given back; one `RGBA8Unorm` texture in private memory; one frame waiting. `present` merges a waiting frame's damage into its own and frees its slot, waits on a condvar when the ring is full (the producer, never the main thread), copies the damaged rows with the lock dropped, then leaves the frame waiting and raises `Pending`. The copy counts its bytes by thread (`MainThreadMarker`), which the check reads. A ring Metal has no memory for stages nothing rather than wait for a slot that never comes.
1012. **For veto:** the main thread's hand-off is `Shadow::take`: it tries the buffer map's lock and each buffer's, takes every waiting frame (placed or not, so no ring fills unseen), and keeps each buffer's texture as last seen, so a frame whose try failed still draws the placement from the texture it had, and the frame shows on the next wakeup, which the producer's wake brings. The slots go back from the command buffer's completed handler on Metal's thread (`release_after`), not from the main thread, so the main thread takes no lock a producer can hold even to release; `present`'s `waitUntilCompleted` stays, so the atlas path's invariant (metal.rs's header) holds unchanged, and a frame that fails to encode drops its blits' slots with their rows (the GPU is gone then).
1013. The blits ride the frame's own command buffer, a blit encoder before the render pass, so a transaction present during a live resize carries its pixels; a buffer shows nothing until its first blit after `create` or `resize` (988's rule, kept by `shown`); `destroy` frees the texture and the ring, lets a waiting producer go, and raises `Pending` too, since a wake alone takes no turn of core: the check first found the placement staying on screen until the next frame without that.
1014. `window::Pending`, an `Arc<AtomicBool>` cloned into the sink: `Shell::settle` draws when it's set as when a turn ran, `Shell::draw` takes it first thing, whether or not a frame follows (an occluded window's pixels wait in the ring, superseded and merged, and the frame drawn on uncovering carries them), and skips the unchanged-scene check when it was set; `Host::sleep` reads it beside `pty_output_waiting`. The Linux stub has no sink, so the flag stays down there and core says it shows no pixels.
1015. The drag per sample: `Pointer::moved` emits a `Drag` on every `CursorMoved` while a button is held, and a `Move` per cell as before, so a text window's selection costs nothing new for a sample inside its cell (the unchanged-scene skip) and die-term's SGR-pixels mode (751) gets pixel steps. Unit test in `tests/input.rs`.
1016. The `pixels` check (S1's check 3 on DIE's own path), under `DIE_UNSEEN=1`: a surface of the check's own places a buffer the size of its area at z −1, a producer thread presents 240 frames 4 ms apart, and the drawable is read back and held to the last frame's pattern and to the offscreen path. On this machine, debug build, three runs: presents=240 frames=240 superseded=0 ring_waits=0 main_bytes=0 producer_bytes=385,228,800 blits=240 buffer=836x480 placed_px=401,280 wrong=0 pixels_differ=0 frame_p99_ms=3.1 to 3.3 worst_turn_ms=9.8 to 11.2 gone=yes (one run under another builder's compiles: frame_p99 34 ms). `idle`, `resize`, `scale`, `show` and `redraw` pass as before; `occlusion` fails unseen on the integration tip too (its cover can't cover an unseen window), and is `DIE_ON_SCREEN`'s. The check isn't in `apps/die-dev/tests/window.rs` yet (the whiteboard package owns die-dev this round); the numbers above are its first limits.
1017. Budget (`cargo xtask budget`): neutral 2,303 → 2,378 (limit 2,380), checks 733 → 891 (895), platform stub 174 → 216 (220), `platform::macos` 1,333 → 1,686 (1,690): the eighth raise, 353 lines, about 290 of them `pixbuf.rs` (the row copies into shared buffers on another thread, the blit, two `Send` wrappers since objc2-metal 0.3.2 marks neither `MTLBuffer` nor `MTLTexture` so, and the ring's bookkeeping) and the rest metal.rs's per-placement binding; `xtask/src/budget.rs`'s pin moves with it. The macOS build is 4,955 of guard 7's 5,000: it holds, with 45 to spare, more than the brief's 150-250 expected, so the next die-render growth on macOS comes out of m0-plan's cut list or M3's own raise. The ring's six unit tests sit in `pixbuf.rs` under `cfg(test)`, which the counter leaves out, since they need its private state. `Cargo.toml` turns on objc2-metal's `MTLBlitCommandEncoder`.
1018. Open: the Linux ring (wgpu, `write_buffer_with` and `copy_buffer_to_texture`, platforms.md §2.1) and the `pixels` row in die-dev's window test; the check ran a debug build, and the release numbers are the hand run's; a producer thread's resize is one frame behind, as the contract says, and shows the old texture's top-left until its next present; a frame that fails to encode loses the blits taken for it; images, source rectangles, offsets and `Hide` placements, with M3.

**die-whiteboard: the pixel stage**

1019. The package: A9's `pixels.rs` on M3a's two halves (the core's at 20b7aca, the render's at 1314627): the whiteboard drawn by tiny-skia, through `canvas.rs` as before, into one pixel buffer the size of the window's area (`Ctx::pixel_buffer` from the `Resize`'s cells, `Producer::resize` when they change), placed under the area's glyphs (`Frame::place` at z −1) with the labels still cells over it; `+` `-` `0` and Cmd+wheel zoom; the wheel pans; cells where the host shows no pixels or your option `pixels` is false. Nothing in core or die-render changed; the cell renderer, the model, the tools and the keymap are as 994-1007 left them, but for the two items below.
1020. The renderer's shape: a **committed canvas** (the board and the selection's outlines, no gesture) and the **shown buffer**, the producer's, which is the committed picture with the gesture drawn over it. A pointer sample during a pen stroke draws only the segments after the ones drawn and presents their rectangle, 0.12 ms on the 1,000-stroke board; the highlighter, a shape and the marquee are put back from the committed canvas and drawn whole each sample (a highlighter drawn segment by segment doubles its alpha at every joint). A committed change draws its region again into a canvas the region's size pasted over (elements culled by their bounds); undo, zoom, a resize, a theme change and the first draw draw it all; a wheel pan moves the picture by whole pixels (`pan_cells` rounds to them) and draws the strips it uncovers, 0.6 ms whatever the board. `render::Renderer` grew `update(&Scene)`, `place(&mut Frame)` and a `Ctx` on `resize`; `Damage` grew `View`.
1021. **For veto:** the pixels are drawn in the event's own turn (`Renderer::update` from the surface's pointer path), not in `render`: `Shell::draw` takes `Pending` before it composes the scene, so a present during `render` would owe a second frame per sample, where one from `event()` rides the frame the turn draws anyway. A command's change (undo, zoom, a key) is drawn in `render` and costs that extra frame, which a key can afford.
1022. **For veto:** a board chooses its renderer at its first event, not when it's made: `Ctx::with_config` restores the session (ext.rs:687) before `window::run` connects the host's sink, so a board restored at startup asking `pixels_supported()` at creation got cells (tests/session.rs found it). Contract §11's "before any extension can ask" doesn't hold for `restore_session`; for M1's list: either the host's sink comes in through `Config`, or the restore runs on the first turn.
1023. **For veto:** damage is a region nearly everywhere now, where 994-1007 said `All`: selecting and deselecting (the old and new selections' bounds), a move under way (before and after each sample), undo and redo (the entry's elements' bounds), nudges, restyles, duplicates and label edits; the view's changes are `Damage::View`. Only a zoom, a resize, a theme change and the first draw draw it all. Without this a selection dragged over the 1,000-stroke board drew it all again per sample, 95 ms in release. With it, `Element::bounds` of an arrow includes its head (0.4 of the head's length to each side, the whole head when the line is shorter), so a region and the file's picture box cover what's drawn; the cell renderer drew the head as a glyph and never noticed.
1024. A full redraw of a dense board runs in bands, one thread each, from 64 elements (`std::thread::scope`, up to 8 bands of 64 rows or more, pasted in): tiny-skia's stroker costs about 0.1 ms per 20-point path with round joins, so the 1,000-stroke board took 95-117 ms on one thread in release and takes 26.5 ms in bands. The present and everything else stays on the main thread, as 6 of the settled calls has it.
1025. The numbers (tests/perf.rs, 1,000 strokes of 20 points, 580 KB of SVG), release: a pointer sample 0.12 ms (its segment rasterized and presented), a full re-raster 26.5 ms, a wheel pan 0.6 ms, a key 0.4 ms, open 37 ms (the first draw included); the test profile (opt-level 1): 0.14, 33, 0.9, 0.3 and 46 ms. In cells on the same board: a full render 1.2 ms, open 8.6 ms. Main-thread work per sample is two orders under the 8.3 ms refresh; the full redraw is what a zoom step costs on a board that dense.
1026. The empty board presents nothing: a fresh buffer is transparent, and the first present carries only what was drawn, so opening or resizing a board copies no transparent pixels into the ring.
1027. Tests: `tests/pixels.rs` (12: the placement's geometry and z and the buffer's size; a drag's ink opaque on its line, anti-aliased at its edges, premultiplied, and nowhere else; each sample's present is the new segment's rectangle alone; a resize re-makes the buffer and a kill destroys it; undo, redo and the eraser in the pixels; zoom about the pointer and the keys; a wheel pan against a fresh redraw; the highlighter's joints and a shape following the drag back; labels as cells with a selected one reversed and its outline in the pixels; two windows placing one buffer; a theme change recolouring the ink; cells when asked), `tests/render.rs` on cells by the option, the tools and session tests on pixel assertions where they read cells, `tests/perf.rs` for both renderers, and `apps/die-dev/tests/whiteboard.rs` end to end through your config at 19 × 40 cells: `\w`, a stroke in mono-dark's red, a rectangle, an arrow, a label, `E` png decoded, `\z` widening the buffer to 120 columns, a split placing the buffer twice, and two restarts as `:rebuild` makes them with the board back drawn and working.
1028. tiny-skia's anti-aliasing isn't bit-exact under a whole-pixel translation (f32 into fixed point): after a wheel pan, a redraw from scratch differs at about 20 edge pixels of thousands by one to three coverage steps, which the eye can't see; the test allows under 2 % of the inked pixels to differ, by 64 at most.
1029. The real binary, unseen: `die --check pixels` (the render half's check) on this tree, debug build, twice: presents=240 frames=240 superseded=0 ring_waits=0 main_bytes=0 blits=240 wrong=0 pixels_differ=0 frame_p99_ms=3.14 and 4.63 worst_turn_ms=12.7 and 10.3 gone=yes. The whiteboard itself isn't driven on the real binary: a die-render check can't depend on an extension (D14), the macOS build has 45 lines left under guard 7, and the check remote injects keys only (`Shell::point` exists for a pointer, but a check that opens a board by its command name would still be die-render knowing an extension). So the GPU path is the render half's check and the extension's path is the harness's fake sink, as the task allowed.
1030. Open: labels are cells, so they don't grow with the zoom (their corner follows it, their outline grows); two windows on one board share one buffer sized by the window resized last, so the other shows its top-left part (C10); Linux draws cells until the wgpu ring; the bands spawn threads per full redraw, no pool; `pixels.rs` is about 430 lines and the crate 4,599 budget lines (uncapped).
1031. The gate on this tree: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo xtask lint` (12 crates), `cargo xtask sources` (278 files), `cargo xtask budget` (die-whiteboard 3,864 → 4,599, uncapped; nothing capped moved: die-core 15,747 of 15,755, die-render 5,171), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`, `cargo xtask cross-check` on x86_64 and aarch64 Linux, and `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` in three chunks: 1,857 passed, 0 failed but xtask's `the_restart_loop_finds_equal_digests_and_logs_each_time` (a restart held to 1 s took 1,021-1,054 ms with other builders on the machine, load 9-15) and `two_launches_leave_one_instance`, which inherits its poisoned mutex; the integration tip 1314627, built in a second worktree and run right after, failed the same way at 1,028 ms. Nothing here is on the restart path, and the loop's session holds no board.

**M3a and die-whiteboard: the review's fixes**

1032. The package: the review's 21 findings on M3a's two halves (981-993, 1008-1018) and the whiteboard (994-1007, 1019-1031), each verified by its repro on the harness or in code; the fixes below, each with a test that fails with the fix taken out (run so, one by one); three skipped and said why. Nothing new in scope: no core primitive, no tool, no key beyond Evil's own reflexes.
1033. die-render, the occluded present (finding 1, held): `Shell::draw` took `Pending` before the occlusion hold, so a present that changed no cell was lost when the uncovering frame's scene matched the last one's. `dirty` and `stale` are `window::Due` now, and a frame held back (occluded, no drawable, no window) after taking the flag leaves the pixels owed as `stale` (`Due::held`), so the frame that follows draws whatever its scene says; the flag itself stays down, so the loop sleeps meanwhile. Unit test in tests/host.rs; the sequence itself wants a covered window, which no unseen check can make (1016's `occlusion`).
1034. die-render, two presenters of one buffer (finding 2, held: two threads at 2048² behind a barrier hung in the first rounds, a slot lost between the copy and the hand-off): each buffer has a `presenting` lock a present holds from its first look at the ring to its hand-off, producers' only, so two take turns and the later supersedes the earlier as one thread's would. The type still allows two (`PixelProducer: Sync`, `present(&self)`); contract §11 says one presenter a buffer is the shape. Test: 2 × 12 presents, no take between, both finish and every slot is free or waiting.
1035. die-render, the lock rule's exceptions (finding 3): said as built, in pixbuf.rs's header, contract §11 and platforms.md: a frame whose encode failed drops its blits on the main thread, which waits for `st`, held by a producer across a few lines or a Metal allocation; a producer that is itself the main thread waits on a full ring like any producer, which `present`'s own wait for the GPU makes rare; neither can cycle. No code.
1036. **For veto:** no byte ceiling under `PIXEL_MAX_SIDE` (finding 5, skipped): a 16,384² buffer is 1 GiB in core (`vec!` aborts if it can't) and up to 4 GiB in Metal, and a sink that gets no memory shows nothing without saying so. Left as is: the cap is a number for you (the display's size × scale, as B4 had it, or bytes), `try_reserve` can't be tested where memory overcommits, and both users size to their area. Contract §11 says so.
1037. die-render, `Shadow::take` (finding 4, held): a frame waiting on a buffer whose texture Metal couldn't make left the shadow's last-seen texture standing, so the placement kept drawing the old size; `*texture = None` before the `continue`. Test: a resize past Metal's side cap, then a present: nothing blitted, no texture, the slot back.
1038. die-render, the completed handler (finding 10): `release_after`'s block runs its drop in `catch_unwind(AssertUnwindSafe(..))`, platform/mod.rs's rule for Rust a framework calls; nothing in it panics today, so no test reaches it.
1039. die-render, the `pixels` check (finding 12, held): `frames >= 1` became `presents <= frames + superseded` and `frames <= presents + 8`, so one frame per present taken is held, not logged. Unseen on this tree, debug, twice: presents=240 frames=240 superseded=0 ring_waits=0 main_bytes=0 blits=240 wrong=0 pixels_differ=0 frame_p99_ms=3.35 and 3.32 worst_turn_ms=12.2 and 9.7 gone=yes. Its row in apps/die-dev/tests/window.rs runs on screen only, so it waits for a hand run (1016).
1040. Finding 6, held as wording: a text window's drag now costs a core turn (`pointer_drag` sets the same selection again), an idle restart and a scene compose, about 0.3 ms, per pointer sample inside one cell, where the sample was dropped before; only the frame is skipped. 1015's "nothing new" was the frame alone. Left as is: a Drag per sample is the contract's shape (§11), and 120 composes a second while a button is held is activity, not idle work.
1041. die-core (findings 8, 9, 11): `PixelRect::union` saturates as `clipped_to` does (a probe at `u32::MAX - 1` panicked under the test profile); `pixel_buffer` sweeps the liveness map of dropped buffers, so producers dropped without a placement (die-term's images will be) don't stay for the workspace's life; and the root names: `Producer` → `PixelProducer`, `MAX_SIDE` → `PIXEL_MAX_SIDE`, `SinkCall` → `PixelSinkCall`, since at `die_core::*` neither Metal's cap nor a test enum said what it was of (earlier calls keep the names as written). The three `Pixels` types stay: core's is `pub(crate)`, the whiteboard's its own, die-render's the host's. die-core 15,755 → 15,765, pixels 195 → 205.
1042. Finding 7 (the sink after the restore), held, core unchanged: contract §11 says the sink comes after `Ctx::with_config`'s restore, so `restore_session` sees `pixels_supported()` false and a surface asks at its first event (1022); M1 carries the sink in through `Config` or runs the restore on the first turn. A fix here is die-render's sink made before the app's `Ctx`, through die's main and the config apps.
1043. die-whiteboard, the pointer (findings 13, 14, 16, held, each reproduced on the harness): a press that ends a label does nothing else (the click away left a dot; `t` and a double click left one too), nor does the right button's; a gesture cut short keeps what it did: a tool key, `u`, `C-r` or the focus going (Cmd-Tab) mid-move or mid-erase ends it as a release where it stands would (`finish_gesture`), logged, dirty, the autosave armed, where before the move stayed unlogged and unsaved and the erased were gone for good with `u` undoing something else; `Esc` mid-gesture takes it back (`cancel_gesture`: the move returned, the erased put back through `undo::revert`, nothing logged). And the press that activates the app: a `Focus(true)` with no press having brought it (the press that focuses a window from another of DIE's comes first, swallowed) arms a 150 ms grace, and a press within it is that click; macOS activates and delivers the click in one burst, so the grace is for the clock between the two, not for a hand. 998's veto item is closed.
1044. die-whiteboard, the hand-over that can't write (finding 15, held: a read-only directory, two strokes, `:rebuild`, the stale file back without a word): `save_session` keeps the board's SVG and the error in `Kept.state` when the write fails, and `restore_session` puts it back over the file's, unsaved, with the error in the status line, a message, and the autosave armed to write it once it can. `:bd` with a failing write still kills the buffer after a message (open: a kill can't be refused from `on_buffer_killed`).
1045. die-whiteboard, the rest (findings 17, 18, 19, 20): a held key repeats into a label (`KeyKind::Repeat` reaches the surface's text, as keys.rs says keymaps match); a region redraw runs in the bands a whole one does, so a sample dragging a selection of 1,001 strokes costs 54.6 ms in the test profile against 170 ms on one thread (perf.rs prints it and holds it under 500 ms); the pixel buffer grows and never shrinks, so a split into windows of two sizes re-makes nothing (a test holds `C-w s`, `C-w v` to no `Resize`; five before) and `\z` back shows the top-left part, C10 refined; the `Board:` prompt lists no `NAME.export.svg`; `ZZ` (`whiteboard-save-and-close`: write, then `vim-quit`), `ZQ`, `<C-^>` and `<C-o>` are in the kind map, to die-vim's commands. `:w`, `:wq` and `:x` still say the buffer has no text: die-vim's write wants a text buffer, and a save hook for surfaces is M1's; using-die.md says so.
1046. **For veto:** labels stay append-only (finding 21, skipped): a caret in a label, `<Left>`, `<Del>`, `<C-w>`, `<C-u>` are a label-editing model (the caret in the model and its cell, Evil's insert keys over it), more than a review fix; the brief's labels had `S-RET` and `BS`. In using-die.md's "what isn't there yet", with the double click on another label while typing one, which ends this one and leaves a dot.
1047. Budget: die-core 15,757 of 15,765, pixels 198 of 205; die-render neutral 2,393 of 2,400 (`Due`), checks 898 of 905, platform::macos 1,693 of 1,700 (the ninth raise, 7 lines: `presenting`, the shadow's `None`, the handler's catch; xtask's pin with it); the macOS build is 4,984 of guard 7's 5,000, so the next growth there is m0-plan's cut list's. die-whiteboard 4,706, uncapped.
1048. The gate on this tree: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo xtask lint` (12 crates), `cargo xtask budget` (all within limits, product code 34,685), `cargo xtask sources` (278 files), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`, `cargo xtask cross-check` on x86_64 and aarch64 Linux, and `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast` in three chunks under `DIE_UNSEEN=1`: 1,867 passed, 0 failed, 40 ignored (die-core 792; die-vim, die-term, die-test-cap, evil-oracle, die-whiteboard and die-orchid-client 632; die-render, die, die-dev and xtask 443, xtask's restart loop included this time). The real binary unseen: `die --check pixels` twice (1039). Nothing shown on screen; the owner's checkout untouched.
1049. Open: a byte ceiling for pixel buffers (1036) and the sink before the restore (1042) are M1's; the `pixels` row in die-dev's window test waits for a hand run on screen; two threads presenting one buffer are serialized but still allowed by the type; a text window's drag composes a scene per sample (1040); `:w` on a board (1045) and label editing (1046) wait for a surface save hook and a label caret; `:bd` on a board whose write fails loses it after a message (1044); while typing a label, a double click on another ends this one and leaves a dot; the macOS build is 16 lines under guard 7, so die-render's next growth there is the cut list's; the buffer's grow-only size means a board narrowed by `\z` rasterizes the wider area still, out of sight.

**The gate on d74e323: a read-only point**

1050. The gate's property test (`cursor_never_hidden_and_render_agrees_with_motions`, its seed kept in prop.proptest-regressions) found a latent core bug, which be31645 has too: a read-only range that an edit had emptied, or one made empty, still blocked a `User` deletion spanning its point, and rode along with insertions at it, so an undo across the point went through and the redo of the same step was refused (`ReadOnly`). Fixed in `blocks`: an empty range protects no text and blocks nothing, as C14's "strictly inside" already said for insertions; a test in edits.rs fails without it. Not this round's: nothing in it touched text or undo.

**The tool dial: Space and the right button**

1051. The package: after the first diagram, a key held that opens a radial dial, the pointer's direction picking the tool and the key's release arming it; minutes later, the right button as the same dial, so a mouse alone draws and switches tools. Built in extensions/die-whiteboard/src/dial.rs (the geometry, the sectors, the hit test, the names' places), with the surface opening, hovering and closing it, both renderers drawing it, one core rule (1052), and tests/dial.rs on the harness. Nothing touches the model, the file or the undo log: a dial round leaves one entry, the drawing's.
1052. Core, contract §7's own rule completed, not a new primitive (guard 5): a release went where its press went only in passthrough (`passed`, filled by `dispatch_passthrough`); now a key press the surface's `event()` takes in any other state (step 6 of the lookup order, returning true, or a key it declares through `handles()`) records `(buf, ev)` the same way (`passed_to`, the same cap of 32 and the same retain on the next press), so its release routes to that surface through the check at the top of `dispatch()`, whatever has the keys by then. A key a keymap took records nothing; releases still never reach keymaps or `observe_keys`. Three cases in tests/surface.rs, failing without it: a key the surface takes hears its repeat and release; one the kind keymap binds doesn't; a release after the focus moved still goes to the pressing surface. Second users: anything that reads a key held in normal state (scope §9's games and viewers); die-term's copy state doesn't need it. Budget: "ext, dispatch, rt" 1,650 → 1,655 (1,649), die-core 15,765 → 15,770 (15,763).
1053. **For veto:** the right-drag eraser is gone. The right button held is the dial: it opens where the button goes down, the drag hovers, the release picks, and a right click without motion changes nothing and erases nothing. The eraser is `e` and the dial's west sector, which a mouse-only hand reaches from the dial; the eraser test in tools.rs says a right drag across a stroke leaves it.
1054. The dial: eight sectors of 45°, pen north then clockwise rect, ellipse, arrow, line, label, eraser, select (the highlighter stays on `h`, a pen mode). It opens about the pointer's last place over the board (the surface tracks every pointer event but the wheel; the area's centre with none), in board units as the marquee is, sized from the cell at the view's zoom so it's the same on screen at any zoom: the dead zone a cell's larger side, the disc three cell heights in radius. The names go on whole cells rather than at a radius (two rows up and down, three columns out, by the end that faces the centre), since at a radius the diagonal names landed on the north and south names' rows; they keep their row wherever in a cell the centre falls and read the same in both renderers; the longer ones (ellipse, eraser, select) reach past the rim.
1055. Picking: Space's release, or the right button's, arms the hovered tool as its key would (`set_tool` for the pen, the one-shot shapes, the label and select; `toggle_tool` for the eraser); the dead zone picks nothing. A left press while it's open picks and is swallowed (`Gesture::Swallowed`, no dot). It closes on Esc (`whiteboard-escape`), `Focus(false)`, a resize, any of the board's own commands (`change()` closes it before the command runs) and any key press that reaches the surface; a key another extension binds (`:`, `C-w …`) runs under it, and Space's release still picks. Repeats of Space are swallowed; Space mid-gesture or while the right button holds the dial, and a right press while Space holds it, do nothing; the wheel waits while it's open; Shift-Space and any modified Space are nobody's, for the second dial. Space while a label is typed types a space, as before.
1056. Rendering: in pixels.rs the dial is a live thing like the marquee (`Thing::Dial`, `LiveKey::Dial(centre, hovered)`): a hover change puts the disc's rectangle back from the committed canvas, draws the dial again and presents that rectangle (124 × 124 px at 2x in the tests), never the picture's; it doesn't go through `Damage`, which redraws the committed picture under it, so "never All" holds by construction. `Target::dial` keeps tiny-skia behind canvas.rs: the disc at 0.12, the hovered sector at 0.35, the spokes and the dead zone's circle at 0.5 of the default foreground, a hairline wide; the names are cells through `Frame::text`, the hovered one reversed. In cells: the names and a `+`. Cost on 1,000 strokes, test profile: a hover change 0.65 ms in pixels (a full re-raster is 28.6 ms there) and 0.13 ms in cells; perf.rs prints it, holds it under 100 ms, and checks the pixel present is the disc's rectangle.
1057. describe-key: Space is declared through `handles()` in normal state, so `\hk` then Space says it goes to the whiteboard surface, which handles it. The line starts with a blank: `key_notation` writes a plain Space as a space, since it's a macro register's text, and the `Surface` trait has no way to name what a declared key does (a `describes()` would be a new primitive). Both in using-die.md's "what isn't there yet".
1058. In passing, a whiteboard bug: `zoom()`'s default centre multiplied the area's size by the view's factor, which has the zoom in it, so `+` at 1.25 zoomed about a point past the centre. `area_centre_px` is the area's physical centre now, the dial's fallback centre too; tools.rs's wheel test holds the point under the centre across a second `+` and fails with the old formula.
1059. Left for later (**for veto**): inks and widths on a second dial (Shift-Space); a configurable key; the wheel while the dial is open (it waits); the dial under a `:` prompt or another extension's command.
1060. The gate on this tree: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo xtask lint` (12 crates), `cargo xtask budget` (die-core 15,763 of 15,770, "ext, dispatch, rt" 1,649 of 1,655, die-whiteboard 5,029, uncapped), `cargo xtask sources` (280 files), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`, and `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on die-core, die-vim, die-term, die, die-dev, xtask, die-whiteboard and die-orchid-client in three chunks under `DIE_UNSEEN=1`: 1,609 passed, 39 ignored (die-core 794; die-vim, die-term, die-whiteboard and die-orchid-client 599; die, die-dev and xtask 216), and two failures that are the load's: xtask's `the_restart_loop_finds_equal_digests_and_logs_each_time` took 1,008-1,080 ms against its 1 s with the load average at 13-18 and poisoned `two_launches_leave_one_instance`'s lock; alone here it passed once and failed once, and on origin/m0/integration untouched, under the same load, it failed three of three (1,008, 1,020, 1,030 ms), as 1048's note on that test has it; `two_launches` passes alone. Nothing shown on screen; the owner's checkout and running DIE untouched.
1061. The review of 5e365d7 found five; each held in code, and each fix has a test that fails on the tree without it (checked by reverting the sources under the tests). The first: 1052's rule recorded a press on `handles()` declaring the key as well as on `event()` taking it, and die-term declares every non-Cmd key (for passthrough) while taking none in copy state, so an unbound key there (F12, Insert, a non-ASCII character) sent a kitty-flag-2 program its release with no press, `\x1b[24;1:3~` for F12, against §7's "a program never sees a release without its press". The record is on `event()` returning true alone, as §7's "a press it takes" says; `handles()` is describe-key's, and says so in ext.rs. The whiteboard loses nothing: its `event()` takes Space's press. tests/surface.rs: a surface that declares every key and takes none has F12's press offered and not its release; die-term's terminal.rs, in the release test: F12 in normal state sends the fullscreen program nothing. Contract §7 and ext.rs say it. "ext, dispatch, rt" 1,649 → 1,647.
1062. The second: the dial opened at a stale board point after the wheel or a zoom key moved the board under a pointer that sends no Move (a cell off after one wheel step, about eight cells off after `++`, off the area after a long scroll). The surface keeps the pointer's last place in the area's physical pixels (`pointer_px`, from every pointer event, the wheel's too, taken before the pan) and converts it through the view when the dial opens or picks. dial.rs: after a wheel step the centre is a cell down the board, under the still pointer; after `++` it's the board point under the pointer's pixel, not the old point, and a move north-east of the pointer hovers rect.
1063. The third: a right click mid-stroke cut the stroke short, and twice over. The whiteboard's `Release(_)` ended any gesture whatever the button; and core's `Surfaces::pressed` was cleared by any button's release, so the rest of the left drag went down the text path and the left release never reached the surface at all (with the whiteboard's half alone the stroke never ends). The whiteboard ends a gesture on the left release alone, only the left button starting one. Core, a real bug of its own (the drag from a surface press is its button's, as `pressed`'s doc had it): the pointer is held by `(window, button)`: the first button down holds it until it comes up, a second button's press and release meanwhile are passed on and take nothing, and the same button pressed again begins afresh, its release lost. tests/surface.rs: press, drag, right click, a drag out of the area, release, all six at the surface; dial.rs: the stroke reaches the lift at x = 80, an erase crosses after the click, and the middle button's release ends nothing. "surfaces and PTYs" 1,445 of 1,445.
1064. The fourth: Shift pressed while Space held the dial made each repeat an undefined key (`plain` wanted no modifier, and `key_notation` writes the shifted Space blank, so "  is undefined" at the OS's repeat rate). Once Space holds the dial, its repeats and release are the dial's whatever modifier joined; a shifted Space with no dial of Space's open is nobody's, as before. dial.rs.
1065. The fifth: `C-w w` between two windows on one board closed the dial and armed the 150 ms activation grace, because `focus()` sent `Focus(false)` then `Focus(true)` to the one buffer; `drop_window` did the same closing one of two. Core, a real bug (the buffer never lost the focus, and `set_window_buffer` already treats the same buffer as no change): `focus_events(old, new)` sends neither when both are one buffer, and `drop_window` goes through it, so a program in two windows isn't told it lost the focus and got it back either; die-term's `fit` on `Focus(true)` loses nothing, the windows showing the terminal being the same set. The contract's `Focus(bool)` line says so. workspace.rs: two windows on a surface pass the focus in silence, closing one onto the other too, and a text window's turn is heard; dial.rs: the dial stays through `C-w w`, the release picks, and the next press draws. "workspace, windows, pointer" 1,395 → 1,405 (1,404), die-core 15,770 → 15,780 (15,775). using-die.md's dial row and pointer paragraph have 1062-1065.
1066. The gate on this tree: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo xtask lint` (12 crates), `cargo xtask budget` (die-core 15,775 of 15,780), `cargo xtask sources` (280 files), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`, and `cargo test --no-fail-fast` on the eight crates in three chunks under `DIE_UNSEEN=1`: 1,616 passed, 39 ignored, none failed (die-core 795; die-vim, die-term, die-whiteboard and die-orchid-client 603; die, die-dev and xtask 218), the load average 7-20; xtask's restart-loop test passed this time. Still open from 1059 and 1057: the second dial, a key of your own, the wheel while the dial is open, describe-key naming what Space does. Nothing shown on screen; the owner's checkout and running DIE untouched.

**die-jellyfish: your jellyfish, the buffer DIE starts on**

1067. The package: your ASCII jellyfish as DIE's starting buffer, simplified as you asked (no source listing, no readouts, no command line or terminal I/O: the animal and its effects), on the colourscheme's background, as `extensions/die-jellyfish` with die-whiteboard's wiring: die-core alone (die-vim and die-test-cap for tests) and no crates.io dependency, `[lints] workspace = true`, a budget row with `extension = true` (uncapped), the lint's NEUTRAL list, the workspace's members and default-members, and registered in die-dev. Cargo.lock goes from 251 packages to 252. The source is `jelly/src/main.rs` (1,527 lines). Nothing in die-core, die-render or `die` changes: the starting buffer is your config's startup hook (1075), and `src/sea.rs` imports nothing but std.
1068. **For veto:** what was cut and what was kept. `sea.rs` is your file's simulation and nothing else: `Listing`, the HUD, `Show`, `Panel`, `Live`, `write`, `row_text`, the CLI, `swim`, `main` and crossterm are gone. Always drawn, in your order: the bell, its lip, the arms, the tentacles, the coats and their flash, the swirl, the halos, the sparks, and the opening glint where the animal is about to be (the `t < 0.9` twinkle). The dust and the random four-point twinkles are kept behind the option `water`, on by default. The far-off little jellyfish are behind `shoal`, **off** by default: they're the one effect that isn't this jellyfish or its water, and `shoal: true` in your config brings them back.
1069. **For veto:** `sea.rs` keeps your file's formatting, under a `#[rustfmt::skip]` on its `mod` line: the repo's first whole file out of rustfmt's hands, where its other skips each keep one table or one literal as written. So a diff against `jelly/src/main.rs` shows what was cut and changed and little else. Your comments stay in your voice, and its header keeps your note on the reel that inspired it. `cargo fmt --all --check` passes over it; every other file in the crate is rustfmt's.
1070. The paper is the theme's. Every cell's background is `Color::Default`, and every fade runs to the paper: `dim` is `mix(paper, c, k)`, where yours mixed toward black on a paper of its own (the `0x060d1c` to `0x010206` gradient, which goes). The port's measurements on #1a1a1a, 400 frames at 160×50 over t = 0..44: with your arithmetic unchanged, fading halos and spark tails pass through the paper's colour and end as dark specks on it (7.5 % and 4.4 % of their cells darker than the paper), and 9.4 % of the dust, 3.5 % of the bell and 17.8 % of the swirl fall under a contrast of 1.15; fading to the paper, none does, and the contrasts are what yours were on black (the bell's tenth percentile 2.02 against 2.08, the dust's median 1.75 against 1.66). Nested fades compose (`mix(p, mix(p, c, a), b)` is `mix(p, c, a·b)`), so nothing else in the arithmetic moves.
1071. **For veto:** the light-theme rule, measured and unseen, since mono-dark is the only theme there is. `Tones::for_theme(background, foreground)`: a background whose relative luminance is under 0.4 gives `bright` white and `deepen` 1.0, both identities, so a dark theme's inks are the fade to the paper and nothing more. Otherwise `bright` is the theme's foreground, standing where white stood in the eight mixes toward it (the coat flash and the bleach among them), and `deepen` is 0.62, scaling the pigments toward black: `ramp()`'s results in `tint`, and the literal inks (`0xdce6ff`, `0xdfe8ff`, `0xe8eeff`, the spark, water and shoal lists). On #f5f5f0 with #2a2a2a for the foreground: fading to the paper alone leaves all of the lip and 98.6 % of the tentacles under a contrast of 1.5; with `bright`, the lip's median is 7.66 and the swirl and twinkles are still invisible, being pale pigments; with the deepening too, the bell's median is 4.49, the tentacles' 3.03 and the dust's 1.84, about the dark theme's 1.75. A test holds both papers to it (1083).
1072. **For veto:** the readouts' five rows of margin became two. Your `layout` kept five rows clear for the clock and the two lines of readouts. With none of them, one row stays clear above and one below, and the animal is centred in the height: `spare = rows − 2 − 5·rise` and `lip_row = 1 + max(spare / 2, 0) + 1.8·rise`, the unit sized from `rows − 2`. So in a window whose height binds, it's drawn larger than your program drew it there. `crowded` goes with the readout that read it: when the height bound, `spare` was 0 give or take 1e-15, so float noise flipped it and the lip hopped a row (at 100×40, 13.6 at an aspect of 0.5 and 14.6 at 0.42); `max(spare / 2, 0)` has no hop.
1073. One entry point, and total: a size, a cell's aspect, the tones, the two flags and a time, in; glyphs and inks out. A time that isn't finite is 0, and it's held to 0..1e12; an aspect that's NaN or not positive is 0.5, and it's held to 0.3..=1.0, as your CLI held it; 0 columns or 0 rows draws nothing. The port's sweeps found what that closes, all in builds with overflow checks, which DIE's tests are: `beat * 31` overflowing from t = 1e18, `latest - 1` at t = −1e300, and `stroke_to` never returning at an aspect of 1e9 (a step per half cell of stroke, unclipped). It reallocates only when the size changes, and clears the inks with the glyphs each frame, where yours left an emptied cell its old ink, so a moment is one picture whatever was drawn before it.
1074. **For veto:** when it shows. Every fresh launch with no file named opens on it. A cold start: the jellyfish alone, where `*scratch*` was (267), and no `*scratch*` is made. Over a session that restored one window: it takes that window, the restored buffers all alive behind it, and `q` uncovers the one beneath. Never over restored splits: it would take one window of them, and a window on a surface is dropped at the next restore, so it would cost you a split. Never after a restart (`--handover`, `--restart-loop`, `--restart-after-frame`), which shows what was there, unless it brought nothing back, where the jellyfish stands in for `*scratch*`. Never with a file named, and never under `--check`. The other choice, only when nothing else would be on screen, is safe everywhere and would never show in your DIE.app, whose session restores on every launch. What follows from this one: a file that arrives after the launch finds the jellyfish up and takes its window, so a Finder double-click or `open -a DIE file` that starts DIE, and `die --wait` with no DIE running (which starts one with no files, then sends them), show a frame of it before the file; with `--wait`, the window goes back to it when you're done with the file.
1075. The wiring: `die_dev::greeted(config, &launch)` wraps `core.startup`, running your hook first and returning its result (the jellyfish's error only when yours had none), and then runs `jellyfish` when no file was named (`config.files.is_empty()` as `greeted` is called), at most one window exists (`ctx.window_list().len() <= 1`), and either the launch is fresh or nothing has the focus. `main.rs` calls it in the two real-run arms alone (`die::app::run` and `die::run`), so `die_dev::config()` is what it was: `--check` and every test that builds your config get no jellyfish and no timer (config.rs asserts none at start; the checks' `idle`, `resize` and `occlusion` would fail on a first buffer that moves or has no text). It's core's guarded hook, so an error or a panic in it is logged and DIE still starts. No API was added to core or the facade for it; `die::Config` has no "starting buffer" field, which alone would have given only the narrower choice in 1074.
1076. **For veto:** the whole window's width by default (`full_width: true`), where a board's default is a file's width: the buffer is made with `max_width = None`, since your text defaults' 80 centred columns would stop the water's glitter and the sparks at an invisible wall either side of the animal. `\z` narrows it to a file's width and widens it again, as on any buffer.
1077. The surface: kind `jellyfish`, buffer `*jellyfish*`, normal state, no cursor, and no status of its own, so the status line shows the directory as on any buffer. `render` returns at once when the frame core kept already shows this time, size, cell shape, tones and flags; otherwise it rewrites every cell, an empty one as a blank and the rest as a glyph in its ink on `Color::Default`. The cell's shape is `cell.width / cell.height` from the render's info, 0.5 while no window has said. The tones come from the `default` face (#101010 and #d0d0d0 where a theme names none, as the whiteboard's), read at creation and on each tick and kept in the surface, since `render` has no `Ctx` and there's no theme-change hook (1004): a `set_theme` repaints at the next tick.
1078. The clock: animation time is a `Duration` kept in the surface. Each tick adds `min(now − last, 250 ms)`, your `clock += dt.min(0.25)`, so a pause neither jumps the animal ahead nor replays anything (a new buffer's first tick adds one `TICK` at most, since the launch was such a jump: 1090). `TICK` is 33,333,333 ns: with no display link until M3 a timer is the only driver, and 33 ms isn't a whole number of a 120 Hz display's refreshes, where this is four. The opening plays once per buffer; `q` and then `:jellyfish` play it again.
1079. **For veto:** it rests after five minutes without a key (`rest_after`; `None` never rests). The rule in a sentence: it swims while a window shows it and you've touched DIE in the last five minutes. Awake, the surface holds an `every(TICK)` and an `idle(rest_after)`, so a kill drops them with it. A tick that finds no window on the buffer drops both, and nothing runs while it's hidden. The idle firing drops both and holds an `on_command_ended` instead, whose next call wakes it: a resting jellyfish is a still picture with no timer. It wakes, with the rest not counted, at the `jellyfish` command, at `Resize` and `Focus(true)` (core sends both when a window takes the buffer), at any key or pointer event that reaches it, and, at rest, at the end of the next command a key runs anywhere in DIE, a prompt's answer or a pointer press (what `on_command_ended` hears). What a resting one doesn't hear: keys a terminal in another split takes in insert state, which run no command, and the wheel over another window; core has no hook for those, so beside a terminal it stays still until a command, a press or the pointer over it. `Focus(false)` doesn't pause it: DIE's window opens without the keyboard, and a start screen that waited for the keyboard would open on an empty frame. Nothing runs at registration or init, so a config that never shows it has no timer. What the five minutes bound: 30 wakes a second while it swims, a covered or minimized window's too (its timers fire and nothing is drawn), against S1's idle rule of under one a second. A frame costs the port's 0.25 to 0.44 ms to work out between 80×24 and 300×90 (it's trigonometry, so the size hardly matters), and core about 0.1 ms to compose when every cell changed.
1080. **For veto:** the keys. `q` lets it go (`jellyfish-close` kills the buffer, and its window shows the buffer it covered, or else the one used last, or core's empty `*scratch*`: 1091), and `:jellyfish` brings it back, the one swimming or a new one. Typing keys are undefined on it (`i is undefined`, as on any surface) rather than lifting it as a splash screen's would: your leader's keys and `:e` already put what you ask for in its window, and a key that silently killed it would be the surprise. It takes no leader key. `:`, `ZZ`, `ZQ`, `C-^` and the `C-w` table are in its kind keymap as the whiteboard has them, the table a copy of the whiteboard's less its `Esc` row (1088), since a surface without text inherits none of die-vim's keys (1004) and an extension depends on die-core alone (D14); `C-o` is `C-^` here, where the whiteboard's is die-vim's jump, which on a surface can only say `no cursor` (1087); `ZZ` is `ZQ` here (`vim-quit`), there being nothing to write, so on a jellyfish alone in DIE both quit it, as `:q` does. A plain `Esc` is taken in silence, as normal state's Esc does nothing, and Ctrl-[ with it (1089).
1081. **For veto:** it isn't in the session: nothing of it is saved and nothing restored. Core writes no surface, so its window's record names no buffer, and the next restore shows the buffer used last in its place: `:rebuild` with the jellyfish up comes back on the buffer used last, your cursor where it was, which is the one beneath it unless another was shown or made since it was covered (the jellyfish marks the one it covers as in use: 1091), and a fresh launch after that opens on the jellyfish again (1074). What a window loses by being under it at a quit or a restart is its own jump list, as under a terminal or a board: a window's places are restored only when a window's record names a buffer.
1082. **For veto:** nobody has seen it on screen yet. It was checked as cell data only: glyphs, inks, backgrounds, sizes and timers, in the harness. No test DIE drew it and nothing went on your screen, so the first look is yours: `:jellyfish` after your `:rebuild`, which as a restart shows what was there and no jellyfish (1074), or the next launch with no file named. What only a look tells is open: whether the inks on #1a1a1a read as they did on your terminal's black, the animal's size in your window with two rows of margin, and how 30 frames a second from a timer looks on your display.
1083. Tests, eight, all headless. `extensions/die-jellyfish/tests/`: no size, time or cell shape panics, and every glyph is printable ASCII (a modest sweep, with 0×0, NaN, the infinities, 1e300 and an aspect of 1e9 in it); a moment is one picture whatever was drawn before, with one small golden frame pinning the port; inks stay on the paper's far side and the animal stays readable on #1a1a1a and #f5f5f0 (no ink beyond the paper, the body at a contrast of 1.15 or more); on the harness, the screen is the pure frame at the clock, the whole window's width, on the theme's background, with no cursor, and a light theme re-tones it; it swims while shown, rests after five idle minutes, wakes at a key and leaves no timer when hidden, resting or killed; and, with die-vim loaded, `q`, `:`, `C-w`, a silent `Esc` and an undefined `i`. `apps/die-dev/tests/jellyfish.rs`, through `die::start` over `greeted(config(..))`: a fresh start with no file opens on the jellyfish alone at 80×24 and `q` leaves an empty `*scratch*` and no timer, while a file named, the config `--check` runs and each kind of restart over a session get none, and a restart over the session of a DIE that had the jellyfish alone, which holds no buffer, gets it again; and over a restored session it covers one window and `q` uncovers the buffer, a session saved with it on screen comes back on the buffer beneath with the cursor where it was, and it leaves a split alone. Each of `greeted`'s conditions was taken out in turn, and each time one of the two failed.
1084. Left for later (**for veto**): coming back in its window after a restart (M1's surface restorer); a key of its own; a light theme to see 1071 on; a theme-change hook, where it re-reads the `default` face each tick (1004); the display link, for pacing (M3); a launch that knows a file is on its way (`die --wait`, Finder), to skip the frame of jellyfish in 1074; a window too small for it, where the animal is cut at the edges below about 49 columns or 30 rows rather than drawn smaller (your unit's floor of 11); and the holes your 72 samples a ring leave in the bell at tall cells (the port counted 2 one-cell holes in 90 frames at the largest unit with an aspect of 0.5, and 109 at 0.75).
1085. The gate on this tree: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo xtask lint` (13 crates), `cargo xtask budget` (die-core 15,775 of 15,780, as it was; die-dev 218 → 236; die-jellyfish 1,448, uncapped), `cargo xtask sources` (286 files), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`, `cargo xtask cross-check` (both Linux targets), and under `DIE_UNSEEN=1` `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p die-jellyfish -p die-dev -p die`: 111 passed, 7 ignored, none failed (die-jellyfish 6; die-dev 36, its 7 ignored the window and looks tests that wait for `ci --full`; die 69), then `cargo test -p xtask --lib`: 83 passed, 1 ignored, the load average 2-3. The port against your file, apart from the tests: `diff` of `jelly/src/main.rs` and `sea.rs` read hunk by hunk, every changed line one of 1068-1073's (the paper, `bright` and `deepen`, the flags, the entry point, the two rows) or a cut; and a throwaway that compiled your `main.rs` beside `sea.rs`, yours with only its `dim` fading to #1a1a1a, the three layout lines of 1072, and the listing, the readouts and the terminal taken out, drew 20,205 frames with both (nine sizes from 8×6 to 240×100, five cell shapes from 0.3 to 1.0, t = 0..44 in tenths and eight moments more, the water and the shoal on): 135,683,310 cells, every glyph the same and every ink the same where a glyph is drawn, so on a dark theme the port is your arithmetic to the bit. Not run: the workspace's tests and `cargo xtask ci`, which start real DIE processes (`xtask/tests/app.rs` launches DIE.app unseen) and are the checkpoint's. Of those, the launches with no session and no file now open on the jellyfish; by reading, none names its first buffer, and the restart loop's launches carry `--restart-loop`, so they aren't fresh ones and get it only where nothing came back. Nothing shown on screen; the owner's checkout and running DIE untouched.
1086. The review of f4edf8f found thirteen, nine once the same thing seen from two sides counts once, and all of them low: nothing was drawn wrong, and no timer ran that shouldn't. Each held when a second reader tried to refute it against the code. Six are in code or tests and three are sentences. Each fix a headless test can see has a line that fails on the tree without it (checked by taking each out of the sources in turn under the tests: eleven mutants, eleven failures, each at the line meant for it); the one no test can see is 1090's. The first: the test named for it didn't hold "a moment is one picture whatever was drawn before" (1073, 1083). It took `sizes[i % 3]`, so no two draws in a row shared a size, and `layout` gives new cells at every change of size: the long-lived sea started every draw clean, and with `self.ink.fill(0)` deleted all six tests passed, an emptied cell keeping its old ink against `inks()`'s "0 where there is none" (the surface reads no ink where there's no glyph, so nothing showed). It's `sizes[i / 4]`: four, four and two moments at each size with the paper alternating, so seven draws land on cells the last one used, and deleting any one of the ink, glyph or part clears fails it. sea.rs doesn't change. And three behaviours 1079 states that no test held (the review took each out with all six passing) are assertions in the swim test now: at rest, a key nobody takes (`x is undefined`, which ends no command) wakes it, the pointer on it wakes it, and `Focus(false)` leaves it swimming. The quarter-second cap stays unheld: the harness's clock stops at every timer's deadline, so each tick there is exactly `TICK`.
1087. **For veto:** the second: `C-o`. It was bound to `vim-jump-backward`, as the whiteboard's is, and using-die.md said "back along the jump list". die-vim's `jump` reads the text cursor of the buffer the key was pressed in before it looks at the list, and a surface outside copy state has no text, so on a jellyfish the key could only say `no cursor`, whatever the list held: an Evil reflex failing on the buffer DIE starts on, over your work. `C-o` is `vim-alternate-buffer` there now, as `C-^` is: the buffer used before, which is where the first `C-o` from a buffer just shown would go, and from that buffer `C-o` is die-vim's own again. `\hk C-o` on a jellyfish reads "C-^: show the buffer used before this one". The other choice is no binding, `<C-o> is undefined` as any other key there, if a key that isn't the jump list shouldn't sit on its name. The whole fix is die-vim's: `jump` starting from the window's newest place when the buffer has no cursor, which would also repair a board's `C-o`, bound the same on main and by reading failing the same (die-whiteboard's lib.rs, using-die.md's board row, neither touched here); a die-vim change, for its own call.
1088. **For veto:** the third: `C-w Esc`. The table's `<Esc>` row, copied with the rest, runs `vim-cancel`, which ends in `text_mut` and so in "that buffer has no text": the one Esc after `C-w` was the only Esc on a jellyfish that said anything, and a fed macro stopped there. The row is out, 31 rows of the whiteboard's 32. With nothing bound core drops the prefix and its count and offers the Esc to the surface, which takes it: `C-w Esc` and `C-w 3 Esc` say nothing, and `C-w v` after them still splits. On text the same keys say `<C-w><Esc> is undefined`, die-vim's own table having no such row. A board has the row and says "that buffer has no text" on main (the review ran it); taking it out there wouldn't give silence, a board's surface not taking Esc after a prefix, so the fix for both is `vim-cancel` skipping `forget_goal` on a buffer without text, in die-vim, left with 1087's.
1089. The fourth: Ctrl-[. `plain_esc` compared the raw key, and a real Ctrl-[ arrives as `[` with Ctrl held, which core folds to Esc only in the chord a keymap matches (848): the surface didn't take it, and core said `<Esc> is undefined`, at the repeat rate while held, where Esc was silent and Ctrl-[ on text is too. It reads `KeyEvent::chord` now, as die-term's observer does, so `\hk` agrees. Notation folds before dispatch, so `type_keys("<C-[>")` couldn't see this; the keys test sends the event as the OS does.
1090. The fifth: where the opening starts at a launch. `wake` stamps `last` when the startup hook shows the jellyfish, which is before DIE has a window or a renderer; the first tick runs in the host's first settle and added `min(now − last, 250 ms)`, so the first frame anyone saw was 0.1 to 0.25 s in (S1's exec to first frame: medians 102 to 142 ms, worst 190), the bell already 2.3 to 4 times its starting size, where your program's clock starts at its first frame. 1078's "a pause never jumps it ahead" didn't hold for that one pause. A new buffer's first tick adds one `TICK` at most. No harness test can see it, the fake clock being on time by construction, so the review's probe was rerun on this tree with a real clock (the hook, a wait, then the host's first settle in its order): after waits of 50 to 400 ms the first frame is the one at 33 ms, and under a tick it's frame 0. The other way, a first tick that adds nothing and shows frame 0 itself, moves every frame number in the tests by one for a thirtieth of a second on screen.
1091. **For veto:** the sixth: what `q` uncovers, and what a restart comes back on. `jellyfish-close` only killed the buffer, so its window went to core's choice, the buffer used last that no window shows, and 1074, 1081 and using-die.md called that the one beneath. It isn't whenever a buffer was made after the covered one was last shown: orchid's `*Orchid Log*`, made at a run's first send or log line, so `:jellyfish` then `q` landed on the log (reproduced; `C-^` from a buffer goes there too with no jellyfish at all, on main), or made while the jellyfish was up; and a `die --wait` file finished by `:q`, which stays on screen at the back of the order. Two changes, both in the extension. `jellyfish` remembers the window it took and the buffer that window showed, and `q` in that window shows that buffer, if it's alive, then kills the jellyfish, the kill queued behind core's `Focus(false)` to it in the same turn: killed at once, core reports "that buffer is gone" for the event, which the review's own candidate did, and the keys test holds `q` quiet. It forgets once the window is seen on another buffer, asked at every tick, wake and event, so `:e file`, `C-^`, `q` shows the file, as any killed buffer's window would. And before it covers a window, `jellyfish` focuses it, the focused one already, which moves nothing and marks its buffer as the one in use: `C-^` and `C-o` go back to it, and a quit or `:rebuild` with the jellyfish up comes back on it (1081, corrected); not when the command acts for a window that isn't the focused one, where it would move the command. `q` from a second window on it, and every window but the one remembered, are core's choice as before. What's left: a jellyfish at rest, whose window a file from outside takes while DIE hasn't the keyboard, hears nothing (no tick, no event, no command), so if your first key then is `C-^` back to it, `q` shows what it first covered and not the file. And the root, which is core's: a buffer made in the background goes to the front of the use order (`create_messages` sets `used = 0`, `output_buffer` doesn't), for its own call.
1092. The seventh to the ninth are sentences. "Your `:rebuild` is the first look" (1082, using-die.md, f4edf8f's message): a `:rebuild` is a restart, launched with `--handover`, and a restart shows what was there (1074), so over any session with a buffer in it no jellyfish is made, and following the sentence the first look is at nothing new. The first look is `:jellyfish` after the `:rebuild`, or the next launch with no file named; 1082 and using-die.md say so now, and f4edf8f's message stands as written, this being its correction. "Over whatever the session brought back" (using-die.md's first bullet): over one window and never over splits, as its own section and 1074 had it. And "DIE's window taking the keyboard" waking one at rest: core sends `Focus(true)` to the focused window's buffer alone, so a jellyfish resting in a split that isn't the focused one stays still when you come back to DIE, until a command ends or the pointer crosses it. 1079 had it right; using-die.md says "while the jellyfish's split has the focus" now.
1093. The gate on this tree: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo xtask lint` (13 crates), `cargo xtask budget` (die-core 15,775 of 15,780 and die-dev 236, as they were; die-jellyfish 1,448 → 1,481, uncapped), `cargo xtask sources` (286 files), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`, `cargo xtask cross-check` (both Linux targets), and under `DIE_UNSEEN=1` `cargo test --no-fail-fast -p die-jellyfish -p die-dev -p die`: 111 passed, 7 ignored, none failed (die-jellyfish 6; die-dev 36; die 69), then `cargo test -p xtask --lib`: 83 passed, 1 ignored, the load average about 3. Still eight tests: the fixes are lines in three of them. Not run, as in 1085: the workspace's tests and `cargo xtask ci`. Still open: die-vim's two changes with a board's `C-o` and `C-w Esc` (1087, 1088), the rest case and core's use order (1091), and the look (1082). Nothing shown on screen; the owner's checkout and running DIE untouched.

**Where DIE starts: your home directory**

1094. What you saw: DIE "seems to begin inside of `~/notes/`", and it should start at home. Why it did: three things, each as built. A new buffer takes the directory of the focused one, as in Emacs (scope §5, `new_directory`), so the `*scratch*` core made when the last buffer in your notes' directory went was made there. The session keeps every text buffer's directory and brings it back, at a launch as at a restart, so that `*scratch*` came back there each time, with nothing else in the session. And the jellyfish, made over it at each launch, took its directory in turn, so `\f` and `\e` on the buffer DIE starts on began in `~/notes/`. Under that, DIE.app wasn't at home either: LaunchServices starts an app in `/` (the running one's working directory was `/`), so with no session your status line and `*scratch*` said `/`.
1095. **For veto:** DIE.app starts in your home directory, as Emacs.app does (`emacs.c` goes home when it's started as an app). `die::app::start_at_home`, which die-dev's `main` calls before it builds the config in the arm that runs the instance, changes the process's directory to `$HOME` when it's `/` and `$HOME` is a directory (`die::home_from_root`), so `die::started_dir`, core's own fallbacks (`std::env::current_dir` in `new_directory`, `find-file`, the project finder, die-vim's ex paths, orchid and the whiteboard) and every program DIE runs without a directory agree. Only from `/`: a DIE run from a terminal without the bundle starts where you were, and a `die` that's the client resolves its names where you typed it, as before. A restart's new instance is started by LaunchServices too, and goes home again. `$PWD` isn't set by LaunchServices and isn't touched (`set_var` is `unsafe`).
1096. **For veto:** a launch puts `*scratch*` where DIE started, and a restart doesn't. `die::app::at_launch(config, &launch, here)`, which `die::app::run` applies, adds this before your startup hook when the launch is fresh (`Launch::fresh`: no `--handover`, `--restart-loop` or `--restart-after-frame`, the rule `greeted` had inline). Only `*scratch*`, and only while it visits no file: it's DIE's own buffer, made wherever you were, and in Emacs a launch has it where Emacs started. Its text, cursor and marks come back as saved. A buffer you made and named keeps its directory, as do files, and a restart brings `*scratch*` back where it was, a restart being invisible. The other rule I weighed, every buffer with no file going home at a launch, would move orchid's chats off their projects.
1097. **For veto:** the jellyfish a launch opens on is where DIE started, whatever it covers. `greeted` sets its directory after showing it, so over a session that brought back one window on a file in a project, `\f` on the jellyfish starts in `~/` and after `q` in the project. A `:jellyfish` of yours takes the directory of the buffer you're in, as any new buffer does.
1098. Not changed, and noted: your status line shows where DIE started for every buffer with no file, not that buffer's own directory (`StatusTextFn` gets the name and the file), so a `*scratch*` made in a project says `~/` while `\f` there starts in the project. Emacs's mode line would say the project. That wants the buffer's directory in the status text's arguments, a core API change, and waits for you to say.
1099. Proven by: `die_app_started_in_the_root_starts_at_home_and_started_anywhere_else_stays` and `a_launch_puts_scratch_where_die_started_and_a_restart_brings_it_back_where_it_was` (die's `facade`), `the_jellyfish_a_launch_opens_on_is_where_die_started_and_not_where_what_it_covers_is` (die-dev's `jellyfish`), and once by hand off your screen: a debug bundle of its own id and state directory, started with `open -g -n` under `DIE_UNSEEN=1` over a session holding your `*scratch*` in another directory, ran with `~` as its working directory (`lsof`), and its session then had `*scratch*` in `~` with its text. API: die's `home_from_root`, `app::start_at_home`, `app::at_launch` and `app::Launch::fresh` are new; die-core's is unchanged. Budget: die 872 → 905, die-dev 236 → 241, neither capped.

**die-markdown: a markdown file's rendered view, on `\m`**

1100. The package. You asked for markdown rendering: "for markdown files I'd love to support a md mode. So in md files we can have a keybind <leader>m to show the file in the rendered md view. <leader>m would toggle the view between render and raw". It's `extensions/die-markdown`, wired as die-jellyfish is: die-core alone among DIE's crates (die-vim and die-test-cap for its tests), `[lints] workspace = true`, a budget row with `extension = true` (uncapped), the lint's NEUTRAL list, the workspace's members and default-members, and registered in die-dev. Cargo.lock goes from 252 packages to 255: die-markdown, pulldown-cmark and its `unicase` (1108). How it sits with 2026-11-05, when you said web content is "probably better" left to "an actual web browser": this isn't web content. It's the markdown view the contract's requirements check has had since October (§18: "An extension providing the file-type mode for `.md` text buffers", on face providers and stored Hide decorations), which that day's entry left as a separate, later option. Your browser preview, with mermaid, MathJax and pictures, is still the browser's, and nothing here exports HTML.
1101. **For veto:** the rendered view is the buffer itself, not a pane beside it, and it's for reading. `\m` hides the markup under decorations and styles the text; the text is never changed, so the buffer isn't modified, undo is untouched, the cursor stays on its text, and raw is exactly what it was. Every key that moves, searches, selects or yanks works in either view. While it's rendered the buffer is read-only to you (`Settings::read_only`, given back as it was at raw and across a restart), and that's the review's doing (1122): I first built it editable, and a hidden mark has no cursor position, so die-vim's keys that work on a character work on the marks beside it too. `x` on a link's last letter deleted its address, `~` upper-cased an address you couldn't see, `a` typed outside the link it looked inside of, each with a clean screen. Raw is where you edit, one key away. An editable view is possible later, by showing the cursor's line raw as Vim's conceal does. The other shapes I weighed: a second, read-only buffer of rendered text (it could reflow paragraphs and draw blank rows the source hasn't got, but it's a second buffer to keep in step and to map places through), and export to a browser, which you have.
1102. **For veto:** a file opens raw, as it has until now, and `\m` renders it. Nothing opens rendered by itself, and there's no option for it: say if you want one.
1103. **For veto:** where the key is. `\m` is bound by your config (die-dev's `bindings`), as every leader key is, and in the `markdown` mode's keymap rather than the global one, so it's a key only in a buffer that has the mode: anywhere else `\m is undefined`. The mode comes with a file named `.md`, `.markdown`, `.mkd`, `.mdown`, `.mkdn` or `.mdwn` (the extensions Emacs's markdown-mode takes; `Options::extensions`). `markdown-toggle` by name (`:` or `M-x`) gives any text buffer the mode and toggles it, for markdown in a buffer with no file. The extension binds one key itself, `<CR>` on a link in the rendered view (1114), and takes no leader key.
1104. **API (die-core), for your later review:** `ModeSpec::files(matches)`, contract §8's file-type modes, which were M1's: a registered mode says which files it's for, and a buffer has it from when it comes to visit one (`visit_file`: opened, restored with the session, or saved under that name) and loses it when it comes to visit a file that doesn't match (`:saveas notes.txt`). A matcher's panic is its extension's, and leaves the mode as it was.
