Then a fixer, and this landing. fig/errors merged into `m0/integration` at 186a499 with no conflict: the builder's three commits (26b0dee, ca60a69, 4a373e5), its two look commits from its own renders (f998201, fc2a191), and the fixer's three (22c96ad, b06c560, 0dd15ed). The landing adds one commit of its own (8790547, 1407). `docs/using-die.md`'s "## fig" covers the error figure, its log, the badge and `.fig` files.

**die-fig phase 2b: the error figure (2026-11-22)**

1400. A figure with no good parse yet shows the error figure in place of the chart. That covers a first parse that failed, a missing data file, malformed data, a figure its data can't build, and no rows. A figure that has drawn once keeps its chart while a later parse fails, with a stale badge in its top-right corner (1413), so nothing flashes while you type. The badge goes when the source parses again. This replaces phase 2's banner (1353): `plot::banner` and `plot::empty` gave way to `plot::error_figure` and `plot::stale_badge`, and `Hit::Banner` is gone.
1401. **For veto:** every error has one of four kinds, each with its own glyph. Unreadable is a missing file, a command that failed, or a file too large. Malformed is a JSON or CSV parse error, from the readers, the row readers or `Table::new`. Mismatch is an unknown field or type, text where a number should be, or an unknown figure type, and it's the kind an error gets when nothing knows better. Empty is no rows, rows a transform removed, every series hidden, and the figures that arrive in a later build (1416). Unreadable files are worded plainly: `name: no such file`, `permission denied`, `isn't text (UTF-8)`. A new limit refuses a file over 1 GiB (`source::TOO_LARGE`).
1402. **For veto:** the symbolic figure, centred a little above the middle: the glyph on a faint disc, the status icon with the kind's name as its label (`Unreadable`, `Malformed`, `Mismatch`, `Empty`), the title `Can't draw NAME` (`Nothing to draw in NAME` for empty), the first error with its place (`line 4, column 43: expected ',' or '}'`), and the hint `! details · \v source`. The icon is critical red for the first three kinds and the amber warning for empty, never colour alone. More than one error adds ` · N errors` to the status row. The hint's `\v` is fixed text, drawn for your leader rather than read from the keymap.
1403. **For veto:** the glyphs are muted outlines. Unreadable is a document with a `?`. Malformed is a document whose last line is a squiggle. Mismatch is two jigsaw pieces a little apart, a round tab low on the left one against a round socket high on the right one. Empty is a pair of axes with faint ghost bars. The look review read the first mismatch glyph, a round tab against a square notch, as the letters "D]".
1404. **For veto:** as the area shrinks, the symbolic figure gives way in this order. First the whole message, up to two lines broken evenly, with the hint. Then the whole message without the hint, then one line of it. Then the glyph with the title, only when the title fits whole. Then the glyph with the status and no count, then the glyph alone, then the title alone. Below 260 px across there's no message at all. The look review found the first ladder cutting the message to keep the hint at mid sizes, and every kind a bare grey glyph at 200 × 120 with nothing saying "error". The status now stays at that size: 200 × 120 shows the glyph and `Malformed` with its icon.
1405. **For veto:** text in the error figure is cut at a word. A cut backs up to a word's end when one is within 6 characters and at least half the text stays, and drops a space or `. , : ; ·` before the `…`. That covers the symbolic title and message and the log header's name. The chart's own ellipses are as they were. The look review found a space before the `…` and four dots after a cut at a `.`.

**die-fig phase 2b: the log (2026-11-22)**

1406. **For veto:** the log is every error and warning of the newest parse, in die-draw's monospace, written as rustc writes a diagnostic. Each entry has the kind and the message, then `--> file:line:column`, then the source line with a caret run under the column, clipped to the width with an ellipsis. Then come the notes on what was tried: the path read, the reader, the transform, a command's exit status and the tail of its stderr. The header reads `N errors · M warnings in NAME`, with the keys `! figure`, `⏎ go to` and `j k move` at the right. The kind word and the carets wear a status ink lifted to 4.5:1 contrast on the ground. Paths in the notes are relative to the figure's folder. The entry under the keyboard or the pointer is lit with a bar in its kind's ink.
1407. **For veto:** a narrow log's header keeps the name first. From this landing, the keys give way from the right, `j k move` first and then `⏎ go to`, before the figure's name is cut, so a 400 px log reads `1 error in malformed.json` beside `! figure ⏎ go to`. It read `malformed.j…` with all three keys shown (8790547, with a test). A name too long to fit whole keeps every key that fits. A name that would show fewer than 6 of its letters is dropped.
1408. **For veto:** a log whose errors are all nothing to draw says `Nothing to draw` with the amber icon, and its entry says it once. Mixed with real errors, they all count as errors. The look review found empty labelled a warning in the figure but `1 error` in the log, with `nothing to draw:` doubled in the entry.
1409. **For veto:** a source line too long for the log shifts by the least that shows the caret and 4 cells after it. A caret run longer than the room starts 2 cells in. The look review found a long line shifted far further than it needed, losing its context.
1410. The keys. `!` turns the symbolic figure over to the log and back, and from a chart with a badge it opens the log over the chart, with `!` again closing it. A click on the symbolic figure, the badge or the log's header turns it over too. In the log, `j` `k`, `C-d` `C-u`, `gg` `G` and the wheel move the lit entry and scroll. `C-d` and `C-u` are bound in fig's keymap and do nothing outside the log. `Esc` closes the log before it does anything else. Two review blockers were here. The keys and the wheel scrolled by a layout made without the figure's name, so `G`, `j` `k`, `C-d` `C-u` and the wheel went to the wrong lines. In DIE, `G` lit an entry that stayed off screen. They now move through the log as drawn, and a test walks every entry.
1411. **For veto:** `<CR>` on a log entry, or a double click on one, opens the source in the window as `\v` does and puts the cursor on the error's line and column. While a chart shows with the badge, `<CR>` keeps its chart meaning, the cursor's values; only the log and the symbolic figure jump. The builder's first version jumped whenever the last build had failed. A double click jumps only when both presses landed on the entry. The press that only gives a window focus still counts as a double click's first. The correctness review found that a double click on the symbolic figure jumped to the source when its second press landed on a log entry. Errors still go to `*messages*`.
1412. **For veto:** `!` on a figure that drew but has warnings shows the warnings' log. With none it says `fig: no errors or warnings`.

**die-fig phase 2b: the stale badge (2026-11-22)**

1413. **For veto:** the badge says `Error · line 14` for an error in the figure's own text, `Error · FILE, line N` for one in a data file, `Error · FILE` with no line, or `Empty`. It sits on the chart's grid: its right edge on the chart's right padding, centred on the title's row or the legend's, on an opaque card's ground with a shadow.
1414. **For veto:** where the badge would cover the chart's text, it says less, in steps. First the whole text, then `Error · line N` without the file, then `Error`, then the icon alone. After that it cuts the title back with its ellipsis, keeping at least 64 px of title for the icon or 128 px for the word. Then the icon alone in the plot's top-right corner, which covers data at 200 × 120. Last, the icon in the area's top corner. The badge's own text is never cut. The look review found the first badge covering the legend and the title at small and mid sizes.
1415. Deviation: `stale_badge` keeps its pinned signature, so it reads what to avoid from the scene: its text items and its legend, title and plot regions, not `ChartLayout`. To cut the title, it rewrites that text item in the scene.

**die-fig phase 2b: nothing to draw, and errors found in the data (2026-11-22)**

1416. **For veto:** graphs, stat and meter tiles, dashboards and `run` or `stream` sources say they "arrive in a later build" as the empty kind: the symbolic figure, or a badge over a kept chart. Before, they said so in a banner like 1353's.
1417. **For veto:** a spec chart whose data has no rows shows the empty figure. A chart whose transforms leave every series empty says `no rows after the transforms`, and without transforms `no rows to chart`. It does so only when there are no rules or notes, so a constant threshold alone over filtered-out data still draws. The correctness review found that data a transform empties drew a blank chart.
1418. **For veto:** when every series is hidden, the chart keeps its legend, axes and regions. The empty glyph, the amber `Empty` status, `Every series is hidden` and how to bring them back are drawn inside the plot. A click on a legend key brings its series back, and the middle of the plot is still the plot to the pointer, not the error figure. That's a deviation from the contract, which puts every series hidden under the empty figure. The correctness review's major was that the builder showed no empty kind there, and didn't report it. One of its probes expects the error figure's hit there and now finds the plot's.
1419. **For veto:** a channel with `"type": "number"` over a column of text is now a mismatch error, placed at the offending cell in the data file when there are no transforms, as `latency-ms.csv, line 6, column 22: "timeout" in "ms" is not a number`. Before, the chart silently drew the strings.
1420. **For veto:** a command that ran and printed nothing but whitespace is empty: `!CMD printed nothing`, with `ran …: exit status: 0` and the tail of its stderr. A failing one with nothing printed is unreadable: `CMD failed and printed nothing`. The correctness review found it reported as an empty buffer read as CSV.

**die-fig phase 2b: .fig files open as figures (2026-11-22)**

1421. **For veto:** a `.fig` or `.fig.json` file opens as its figure, a fig buffer in the window as `\v` makes, with the text buffer behind it, and `\v` flips to the text and back. CSV, JSON and JSONL still open as text. A `.fig` reached in the focused window from anywhere except its own figure opens as the figure, unless you last looked at it as text. So once you leave it as text, `:e` opens it as text until you press `\v`. Reached from its own figure (`\v`, `C-o`, `C-^`, or `:b` with its name), it's text. The last view is recorded only for `.fig` buffers, from the focused window after each command, and the session keeps it, so a restart reopens each file as you last looked at it.
1422. That rule is the fixer's. The builder's opened a `.fig` as its figure only when freshly opened or restored with text in it. The correctness review found two majors there. A `.fig` opened a second time in a session opened as text. And the session recorded only `\v` and the jump to an error as the last view, so a restart could reopen as a figure a file you had last seen as text. One of the review's probes expects the figure after a restart where the file was last shown by name as text. It now reopens as text, which is intended, and a new test covers it.
1423. **For veto:** only the focused window flips. The figure takes a `.fig`'s place in the focused window, or later, when the focused window first shows it, rather than in every window showing it, because flipping a window without focus would move the focus. A buffer saved under a `.fig` name, a new empty `.fig`, and one already looked at as text stay text. A `.fig` that fails on opening shows the symbolic figure, and `\v` goes to its text.
1424. The option is `open_fig_as: OpenAs { Figure, Text }`, Figure by default. die-dev constructs `Fig::new(Options { open_fig_as: OpenAs::Figure, .. })` explicitly, so your config names it, and die-dev's tests/fig.rs opens a `.fig` through your config.
1425. **For veto:** `fig-spec` makes a buffer visiting `NAME.fig` in the data's folder, unsaved, so `:w` writes it there. If that file exists, it makes a buffer with no file and says so. `fig-spec` and `E`'s default name drop a whole `.fig` or `.fig.json`: `other.fig.json` gives `other.fig` and `other.png`. The correctness review found `NAME.fig.fig`.
1426. **For veto:** seen, not fixed, as it wasn't a finding: `\v` from a figure made by `:fig rates.fig` goes back to the buffer `:fig` was typed in, not to the file's text. That's the existing order of leaving a figure.

**die-fig phase 2b: four fixes from phase 2's gallery (2026-11-22)**

1427. **For veto:** a bar's card goes to the nearest place clear of every bar, every total and the pointer, and of the crosshair's column on bars over time, 8 px from them. A place costs its distance from where the card would have gone, plus the pixels it rises above the plot, plus a band's step for each band it sits past its neighbour. Places centred on its own band and on its neighbours are tried. The pointer is kept `CARD_GAP` (12 px) clear rather than 14 px, which also stops horizontal bars' cards moving for a 1 px overlap. The card may still sit a bar or two away: the deploys card goes right of the 09:00 bar. It covers bars only when nothing else fits. This settles the card that 1393 left covering a neighbour, as m2's does.
1428. **For veto:** the search tries at most 96 places along the bars' narrow axis, nearest first. That covers every place on charts up to about 48 bars. On denser charts far places may go untried, and the card may then cover bars. The correctness review's blocker was the builder's first search, cubic in the bar count, which froze the main thread when hovering a chart of many bars. A speed test (ignored, as they all are) hovers 2,000 bars. The look review's minor, a card two bands from its own while that band's head was free, is the neighbour cost above.
1429. **For veto:** a scatter fits its data's own extent, and its layout pads that by the dots' reach in pixels, so non-negative data never shows a negative tick. On a scatter's y only, the domain goes on to the next tick when the last tick would label less than 75 % of the dots' range, at the bottom only if that stays at or above zero, and only where the new ticks label the new ends. A plot under 4 times the dots' reach lets dots past its top and bottom. scatter.csv's two negative memory values, −0.14 and −0.08, which no real memory reading could be, are 0.06 and 0.12 now. scatter.png runs memory 0 to 9 for dots up to 8.8. The look review found dots clipped at 200 × 120, and at 400 × 240 only 0 and 5 labelled while the dots reached 8.8.
1430. A number x's card header is its title and value without a colon, `Hour 26`, as quiet as `10:15 · Sat 21 Nov`. 1393 had left `Hour: 26` for you.
1431. A size change drops the hover and its card until the pointer next moves, and the keyboard cursor stays. That settles 1382's card left behind after `C-w v`, inside die-fig and with no core change.
1432. **For veto:** seen, not fixed, as it wasn't a finding: at 200 × 120, thresholds.json and many.json draw overlapping y tick labels (`200` over `0`, `160` over `100`) on plots 10 to 20 px tall.

**die-fig phase 2b: the API, the tests and the review (2026-11-22)**

1433. Deviations in the types. `FigError` has `kind`, `file` and `context` as the contract has them, and two fields beyond it: `warning`, so the log can tell warnings apart, and `line`, the data file's line for the caret, which `error_figure`'s one `source` can't supply. Constructors default the kind to mismatch, and parse errors use a new `FigError::malformed`. `plot::error_figure` takes the figure's name after `source`, for the title and the log's places. `plot::log_layout` takes the name too, which changes its public signature. `source::Failed` is `{ errors, warnings, text, files }` in place of `{ error, file, files }`, the file living on each error. `Built` drops `later`, since later figures fail as empty, and gains `text`. `FigState` drops `later` and `banner()` and gains `badge()`, `warnings()`, `log_errors()`, `log_layout()`, `failed()`, `fail_gen` and `text`. New public helpers: `plot::errors` (`ErrorView`, `LogLayout`, `log_layout`, `log_page`, `log_entries`, `badge_text`, `title`, `summary`, `readable`, `glyph`, `status_icon`), `csv::cell_at`, `plot::not_a_number`, `source::relative` and `source::TOO_LARGE`. There's no die-core change, so docs/extension-contract.md has no new line.
1434. fig_shot draws the error figure for a file that can't be drawn, `--log` its log (`--log-top` the first line shown, `--log-mark` the entry lit), and `--stale BAD` the file's chart with BAD's badge, the positional file being the good one. Example files that fail on purpose are in `examples/data/errors/`: a missing data file, malformed JSON, an unknown field, text in numbers, a CSV with only its header, and a spec with six errors and five warnings.
1435. Tests. tests/errors.rs (18) runs the error figure on the harness with cell pixels: turning it over by `!` and by a click, the jump by `<CR>` and by a double click, every log key and the wheel reaching every entry as drawn, the badge kept until a good edit, a data file's error at its cell, `.fig` files opened, reopened, restarted and saved, `open_fig_as: Text`, data with only a header, a filter keeping no row, and `fig-spec` on a `.fig.json`. tests/plot.rs grows from 61 to 75 with the figure's kinds, ladder, log, badge, cuts and the four fixes. tests/pointer.rs grows from 41 to 42, with the 2,000-bar speed test ignored. tests/live.rs goes from 13 to 9, its four banner tests replaced by tests/errors.rs. die-dev's tests/fig.rs grows from 5 to 7.
1436. The review: 19 findings, 3 blockers, 6 majors and 10 minors. The correctness reviewer's blockers were the bar card's cubic search (1428) and the log's keys and wheel on the wrong layout (1410). Its majors were the second opening and the session's record of a `.fig` (1422), data a transform empties (1417), and every series hidden (1418). Its minors were `NAME.fig.fig` (1425), a silent command (1420) and the double click (1411). The look reviewer's blocker was `G` lighting an entry off screen in DIE (1410). Its majors were the badge over the legend and title (1414) and the message cut for the hint (1404). Its minors were the ellipses (1405), the bare glyph at 200 × 120 (1404), the long line's shift (1409), the scatter at small sizes (1429), empty as a warning and `1 error` at once (1408), the "D]" glyph (1403), and the card two bands away (1428). All 19 are fixed, each with a test.
1437. The final renders are in `die-fig-previews/4-errors/`, with a README: each kind's symbolic figure, the malformed one's log, a long log at its top and scrolled, requests.csv's chart with a bad edit's badge, light, 400 × 240 and 200 × 120, and the four gallery fixes (services-bars and latency hovered, the scatter, the twelve-series card). They were drawn by this landing's tree after the 400 px log's header was fixed. The builder's and the fixer's renders stayed in the scratchpad.

**die-fig phase 2b: what nobody has seen, and the gate (2026-11-22)**

1438. **For veto:** nobody has seen the error figure, its log, the badge or a `.fig` opening as its figure on screen, in a DIE window or otherwise. They were checked as the pixels the harness's fake sink was handed, and as fig_shot's PNGs, which the look reviewer, the fixer and this landing looked at one by one. Every test ran under `DIE_UNSEEN=1`. What only a look and a hand tell: whether the badge's appearing and going while you type a broken line reads as calm or as flicker; whether `<CR>` from the log lands where you expect in the source; whether `:e`, `:b` and a restart bring a `.fig` back the way you last looked at it, as 1421 intends; how the glyphs and the log's monospace sit beside the editor's own text at your cell size; and whether the bar card's new places feel right under a moving pointer. Your first look is `\v` on any file under `extensions/die-fig/examples/data/errors/`, or opening `missing-data.fig` there, after `:rebuild` from `main` once this merges.
1439. Sizes on the merged tree: die-fig 24,420 lines and die-draw 1,809, both uncapped extensions; die-markdown 12,120; die-dev 314. die-core is 15,926 of 15,930 and die-render 5,200 (macOS build 4,984 of 5,000), unchanged, since no file under `crates/` changed.
1440. The gate on 8790547, fig/errors merged with the landing's fix:
- `cargo fmt --all --check`.
- `cargo xtask lint` (16 crates).
- `cargo xtask budget`, as 1439 has it.
- `cargo xtask sources` (390 files).
- `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --locked --offline -- -D warnings`.
- Under `DIE_UNSEEN=1`, `cargo test -p die-core -p die-render -p die-markdown -p die-draw -p die-fig -p die-whiteboard --locked --offline --no-fail-fast`. On the merge, 186a499, every target passed (1,690 tests, 21 ignored) but die-render's tests/keys.rs, whose three tests `the_matrix_holds`, `a_panic_in_the_key_monitor_is_caught` and `a_surface_in_passthrough_hears_repeats_and_releases` got no key events, because this session runs inside a DIE terminal (1383). On 8790547 the same, with 1,691 passing, except that die-core's tests/pty.rs failed once in `nothing_is_left_running_after_shutdown`: its `pgrep` ran before the shell's background `sleep` was there (`its job: ParseIntError { kind: Empty }`). Run alone, it passed three times out of three. die-fig's own targets passed 444 (lib 48, algo 51, data 43, errors 18, keys 24, live 9, plot 75, pointer 42, render 36, scale 44, session 4, spec 38, surface 12) and die-draw's 13, with the 5 speed tests ignored.
- `cargo test -p die-dev --test fig --test theme --test looks --test config --test whiteboard --test orchid --test markdown --locked --offline`: 33 passed (config 17, fig 7, markdown 3, orchid 1, theme 2, whiteboard 3), `looks`' one ignored as ever.
- This entry: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo xtask lint` and `cargo xtask sources`.

Not run: the rest of the workspace's tests, `cross-check`, `clean-build`, `api-diff`, the ignored tests of `ci --full`, `ci --linux`, `mutate`, and S1's on-screen checks.

**die-fig phase 3: what it is and how it was built (2026-11-22)**

1441. Phase 3 is the design study's third: "The graph figure with fig's layout ported, tree and layered layouts for hierarchies, emphasis, node drag, the detail panel, search and the table view." There's still no dial (1194), and the study's m3, the service graph with its detail panel, was the target for graphs and the panel. `\v` on fig's graph JSON, a spec of `"type": "graph"` or a CSV with an `id` and a `parent` column now draws a network graph, laid out once in a job. `⏎` opens a detail panel beside a chart or a graph. `/` lights what matches as you type, and `n` `N` go on from there. `t` gives a graph's node and edge tables, and `s` its labels, its layout and `relayout`. The phase also carries three fixes: `\v` from a figure made by `:fig rates.fig` (1426), tick labels overlapping at 200 × 120 (1432), and the mismatch glyph's right piece poking past its disc.
1442. How it was built. Two builders ran in parallel on code with no editor in it: the graph (P3-graph, fig/graph), and the detail panel with search's model and the table views (P3-detail, fig/detail). The surface's builder (P3-surface, fig/p3-surface) merged both first, fig/graph at 2cbd0e3 and fig/detail at 9194e22, where both branches had changed fig_shot's flags and it resolved them by hand. Then it put both in the editor (59bf119, 1321728, 44c3785). Three reviewers followed, each with probes of its own: one for the look, from fig_shot's renders of the fixtures, dark and light, at several sizes; one for correctness; and one for the interaction, against mouse-ux. A fixer took their findings in two halves, the editor's (ba5c9a2) and the look's (fa84420), then their tests and the guide (fe4af83). The lander merged fig/p3-surface into `m0/integration` at c4bb8e7 and added die-dev's end-to-end test (1670f17). A look pass on the merged tree came last, on fig/look-3 (85c5ffa, a3abbc5, 79eb5ee). `main` is still at phase 2b's merge, 826e2b1. docs/using-die.md's "## fig" covers graphs, the panel, search, `t` and `s`, and its What isn't there yet says what's left.

**die-fig phase 3: the graph figure (P3-graph, 2026-11-22)**

1443. The graph. `GraphModel::build` takes a `GraphSpec` (fig's graph JSON or the grammar's) or a table with id and parent columns. It keeps nodes and edges in source order with their places, and works out once the adjacency, each node's in and out degree, the id-to-index map, each node's radius, and each edge's width and its lane among the edges between the same two nodes. A layout runs once, in the caller's job: fig's stress layout (algo::stress, verbatim), a tidy tree (algo::tree), or layers with their routed edges (algo::layered). `graph::base` draws the edges, nodes and labels at rest, and `graph::overlay` the wash, the hovered node or edge, the rings, the path, the drag's ghost and the card. Hover, the nav, the path by BFS, the two tables and search's names come with it. An edge is 1 px, or 1 to 3 px by its weight when two weights differ. An unknown edge end is a mismatch error at the edge's place, a duplicate id is an error, and self-loops and multi-edges draw.
1444. Fixtures, in `examples/data/graphs/`, all made up: services.json (18 nodes and 25 edges in three groups, like m3), fig-graph.json (fig's own graph shape with fig blocks: colours, `show-label`, `override-focus`; 10 nodes), org.csv (39 rows of id, parent, name, role and group), pipeline.json (a directed graph of 20 nodes in five stages), big.json (300 nodes, 387 edges) and cycle.json (6 nodes, 10 edges, directed with cycles). fig_shot draws graphs: `--cursor-node ID`, `--hover-node ID` (after its dwell, so in focus), `--hover-edge A,B`, `--reference ID` (with a cursor or hovered node, the path), `--pin ID@X,Y` (again for more), `--zoom F`, `--layout force|tree|layered|auto`, `--labels off` and `--ghost` (a drag still under way), and from the surface `--search Q` and `--detail`. `--reference` now takes text, a number on charts and a node's id on graphs.
1445. **For veto:** layout `auto` is the default when a spec names none: a tree when the edges form a forest (at least one edge, no cycle, no pair given twice), layers when every edge is directed and there's no cycle, and stress otherwise; a graph with no edges gets stress. So a fig graph file with no layout that happens to be a tree now opens as a tree, not as fig's stress layout. A `"layout"` in the spec overrides it, and so does `s` (1499). `spec::Layout` gains `Auto`, a variant in a pinned type, because contract-p3 asks that the spec's layout override the graph's own choice. `:fig-spec` leaves `auto` out and now writes an explicit `force`.
1446. **For veto:** a tree from a table. An `id` column and a `parent` column (or `parent_id`, `parentid`, `parent id` or `parent-id`, in any case) make a tree, its label from `label`, `name` or `title`, its group from `group` and its size from `size`. Edges run parent to child, undirected. A row's place is its CSV line (the row + 2), column 1. An unknown parent is a mismatch error at that row's line, and a duplicate id is an error. This builds what 1272 left for phase 3, with a hook in `spec::inferred()` so such a table parses as a graph.
1447. **For veto:** a wide tree, with more than 8 leaves and more than 3 leaves a level of depth, stacks each manager's two or more leaves in a column under it, 0.22 units to the right, with rounded elbow edges. That's what keeps org.csv readable at 880 px. Other tree and layered edges are vertical S-curves. A long layered edge is a B-spline through its routed slots (1297) and never swings past them.
1448. **For veto:** layers now balance. After the barycentre sweeps, 6 sweeps move each node to the median of its neighbours above and below, the order kept, a unit apart at least, on a 1/1024 grid. That moves x in every layered layout, pipeline.json's included, and replaces 1297's layers centred on x = 0 with no further pass, which contract §5.8 had pinned. The look reviewer found a tree laid out in layers with its parents off the middle of their children and its edges sweeping in long bundles. A test in tests/algo.rs that held three nodes' exact positions now holds the property it was checking: the edge from node 12 to 13 passes node 9's layer at least a unit from it.
1449. **For veto:** in a tree or layers, an edge between two nodes of one rank is a cubic arc. It bows to the side where the nodes between have fewer edges, above on a tie, high enough to clear them and at most 0.4 of a rank. The look reviewer found the tree layout, on a graph that isn't a tree, drawing such edges straight through the nodes between.
1450. **For veto:** trees and layers scale across and up separately, capped at 170 and 96 px a unit, so a small tree stays compact. Stress keeps one scale both ways, its layout's aspect as fig-port has it, so a round layout like big.json's or cycle.json's uses only the middle of a wide area (the look pass left that, 1523). Zoom stays between 2× out and 32× in from the fit.
1451. **For veto:** node size is a square-root scale from zero onto a radius of 3 to 9 px (6 to 18 px across), from the `size` field or else the degree, times fig's `size` of 1, 1.4 or 1.8 (ruling 15). When nodes crowd at the current zoom, judged by the median gap to the nearest neighbour, they shrink to as little as 60 % (a radius of 2.6 at least) and grow back as you zoom in. That's what makes big.json at fit read as dots rather than a heap of discs.
1452. **For veto:** edges at rest are the baseline's grey taken 18 % toward muted (#484848 on mono-dark), where contract-p3 said the border's grey, so a graph's shape reads with nothing in focus. A neighbour's edge in emphasis is muted grey and 0.5 px wider, a path's edge the strong text colour and 1 px wider, and a hovered edge the text colour and 1 px wider. Arrowheads are 7 px long, the tip at the ring. Two edges between the same nodes bow 14 px apart at their middles. A self-loop is a teardrop in the middle of the widest gap between its node's other edges, or up and to the right when it has none; the look reviewer found it always up and to the right, over whatever edge left that way.
1453. **For veto:** group colours go to the eight series slots in order of first appearance; in services.json `platform` comes first, so the colours match m3. A node with no group, among nodes that have groups, is neutral; when no node has a group, every node takes slot 1. A ninth group on is neutral (ruling 4). The legend lists the groups when there are two or more.
1454. **For veto:** labels at rest. A label is 11.5 px regular in the text colour (ruling 14); the focused node's is 12.5 px semibold in the strong text. Spots are tried right, left, below and above, then the four corners at 0.4 of an edge's cost, and the look pass added four more (1518). Hubs (degree 4 or more) and `show-label` labels may also try two rings further out, at 15 and 30 edges' cost. A label must be nearer its own node's ring than any other node's, by 4 px or 30 % of its own distance, whichever is more (`OWN_MARGIN`), and two labels level with each other keep 7 px apart. A label may cross 2 edges, on a ground of the surface's colour (a hub as many edges as it has, up to 6); otherwise it's culled, the busiest nodes placed first. Contract-p3 asked for labels that cross no edge where a free spot exists. The look reviewer found labels at the corners as near another node as their own, hubs' labels floating on the far rings, and two labels 2 px apart reading as one phrase. With the margin, dense graphs cull more labels at rest than the builder's did.
1455. **For veto:** labels in the overlay. The focused node's label always shows, on a ground that hides the dimmed nodes it touches (cleared as discs). A neighbour's label that could only show by covering a bright label or node is left out, and that node is lit and named in the card. A dimmed node near a spot adds to its cost rather than ruling it out. Contract-p3 said the cursor's neighbours always keep their labels. The look reviewer's first major was the overlay's labels landing beside other nodes and reading as theirs.
1456. **For veto:** with no subtitle in the spec, the line under the title reads `services.json · 18 nodes · 25 edges · force layout`, as in m3. Without a title it doesn't show, so org.csv shows just its legend.
1457. **For veto:** a node's card has its label as the title, a group row with its dot, a degree row (`degree · 2 in, 3 out` when every edge is directed), `N hops / from REF` while a path shows, then the node's first four other fields, its metadata flattened. Values are semibold and left-aligned, numbers as the data writes them (8080, not 8,080). For a table's row the name column is dropped and the parent is given by its label. An edge's card says `a → b` or `a — b`, then its label, weight and fields.
1458. **For veto:** where the card goes is a cost. It never covers its node. Covering the focused node or the cursor's costs most, then emphasised nodes and their labels, bright edges (a path's most), and the title and the legend; a node at rest costs 6 and the hovered edge 10. Straying from the anchor costs 0.04 a px, and 0.3 a px more past 60 px, with extra tries 14, 28 and 44 px out, and switching sides costs a little, a cell's hysteresis. In a small area the card drops its own field rows, at a cost of 4 each (group, degree and hops are kept), and narrows its columns before it covers a neighbour. A label the card would cut is cleared whole. The look reviewer's majors were a small area's card keeping every row over the neighbours, the title and the legend, and a card straying far from what it's about, as the surface's builder had seen api's card land at the lower left; its minors were dimmed labels cut in half at the card's edges and a hovered edge's card over that edge. At 400 × 240 the cursor's card may still cover the title and the legend, after dropping rows, when the graph leaves no room (1523).
1459. **For veto:** on a graph the cursor's emphasis sticks: its neighbours and their edges stay bright and the rest dims to about 25 %, as in m3, beyond ruling 6's list of what dims (a legend key under the pointer, `o`, a search hit, a graph's hover after its dwell). On charts the cursor still dims nothing. A node brought into focus by a dwell or a search hit takes over, a legend key under the pointer emphasises its group, and a reference with a hovered or cursor node shows the path. `Esc`'s last rung lets the cursor's emphasis go (1489): the ring stays and nothing dims until the cursor moves, a click or a search. A click on the background still does nothing, as contract-p3 §4 says. The interaction reviewer found a graph that stayed dimmed once a node had the cursor, with no key or click to clear it.
1460. **For veto:** a node hovered for 100 ms (`DWELL_EMPHASIS`) comes into focus and lets go after as long, so crossing between two nodes doesn't flash. The emphasis comes and goes at once, with no fade, the same gap as the legend's emphasis on charts (1375).
1461. **For veto:** rings. A focus or cursor ring is 2 px in the strong text colour, 4.5 px outside its node, and the reference's ring is dashed at the same radius. A pin is a small dot in the strong colour at the node's upper right. The look pass gave a ring its own room and put the pin on the ring's line (1515, 1516).
1462. **For veto:** the wash is the surface's colour at 192/255, over only what the graph covers (its nodes, edges and labels) rather than the whole region, about half the raster cost at fit. 192/255 is an exact step, so the host's compositing shows no seam.
1463. **For veto:** with every group hidden, the graph draws the chart's note (1418), which says `Every series is hidden`: series, not groups. `X` or a click on a key brings them back.
1464. **For veto:** the node and edge tables leave out the nodes of hidden groups and their edges, as a chart's table leaves out hidden series. Search's names give each node's label, and its id too where that differs.
1465. **For veto:** a drag pins the node and holds the window at what was shown, so nothing else moves, and a fit afterwards takes in the pinned place. In a tree or layers the node slides along its rank only. It stays inside the graph's region, inset by its radius; the interaction reviewer found a node dragged under the detail panel, where it vanished and couldn't be grabbed again. A node or an edge whose fig block says `hidden` is never drawn, and nothing brings it back.
1466. **For veto:** `]` and `[` go round the anchor's neighbours clockwise from straight up. `Action::Neighbour` takes the first or the last, and repeating keeps the anchor (`nav::neighbour`), so `]` `]` `]` circles one node. `gg` and `G` go to the first and last node in source order. `h` `j` `k` `l` with no cursor put it on the node nearest the plot's middle. A pan keeps the window's middle over the fitted graph. `reveal` leaves the view alone while the node is more than 6 % inside the plot, and otherwise recentres on it at the same zoom (fig-port §7).
1467. Hover takes the topmost node (the last in node order) whose disc holds the pointer; otherwise the nearest node within its radius and 4 px, 12 px at least; otherwise the nearest edge within 4 px.
1468. Above 2,000 nodes the force layout switches to a sparse stress: 64 pivots chosen farthest first, hop springs on the edges, Ortmann's region weights and collisions found from a grid, with fig's steps, cooling and normalising.
1469. **For veto:** the reference's path and the card's `N hops` go only through nodes and edges that are drawn; otherwise there's no path. The correctness reviewer found paths through hidden groups.
1470. **For veto:** `{"nodes": []}` is nothing to draw, the empty kind and its glyph, where it was a mismatch. The duplicate id's message names the id, `node ids must be unique: a is given twice`, where 1264 kept fig's words, which don't.
1471. **For veto:** what a hover costs on big.json (300 nodes; an ignored speed test, in release). The overlay's scene takes 1.22 ms at worst, up from the builder's 0.24 ms since the card's search and the labels' scoring do more, inside contract-p3's 2 ms. Rasterising its damage at 2× takes 4.26 ms (the correctness reviewer measured 4.1 ms), because raster.rs draws every fill through a mask (1289): the wash costs about 1.4 ms and the card's shadow about 0.9 ms. That's accepted against the 8 ms the charts allow a hover move in tests/pointer.rs, not the 2 ms. The fixer tried drawing large fills that sit wholly inside the clip without the mask, and reverted it: masked and unmasked fills differ by one colour level, which breaks render.rs's test that a box redrawn equals the whole draw. A fast path for solid rectangles would be the renderer's to make.
1472. Deviations in the graph. Besides `Layout::Auto` (1445), it made the smallest edits it could in files it doesn't own: spec/mod.rs (the variant, the hook, two doc lines), spec/write.rs, and one assertion in tests/spec.rs, from `Layout::Force` to `Layout::Auto`. The HitIndex lives inside `GraphLayout` (`layout.hits`), so `graph::base` returns `(Scene, GraphLayout)` as contract-p3 has it, rather than plot's triple, and there's no `Hit::Edge`: edges are found through the HitIndex (ruling 5). Signatures pass what they need where the contract wrote shorthand: `hover(model, layout, x, y)`, `step(model, layout, from, Dir)`, `neighbour(model, layout, anchor, current, steps)`, the anchor explicit so the surface keeps it while `]` and `[` repeat, and `drag_node(model, layout, view, id, to, grab)`. fig_shot lays a graph's overlay on its base in separate buffers, as the host does; a chart's preview still draws one flattened scene, where the wash rounds the surface a level off. The test of every fixture at three sizes draws with FixedMeasure and rasterises without fonts; the builder's PNGs, with fonts, stayed in the run's scratchpad.

**die-fig phase 3: the detail panel, search's model and the tables (P3-detail, 2026-11-22)**

1473. The panel. `detail::DetailTree` holds rows of a depth, a key, a value, a kind, the mark the row names (`Item`: a series, a point, a band or its part, a cell, a graph's node or edge) and whether it's folded. Trees are built for a chart's point, series, band or cell (plot/detail.rs) and a graph's node or edge. `detail::panel` draws the tree with a hit region for each row and each fold triangle (`Hit::DetailFold`), and `detail::keys` moves, folds, copies and jumps. `search::Search` ranks named items with fig's score, with the matched ranges and `n` and `N` over them. `tsv_at` gives a band chart a row for every category and a heatmap a grid.
1474. **For veto:** the panel's ground is a step off the surface. On dark it's 40 % of the way to the card's ground, #1e1e1e as in m3; on light, 30 % toward the grid, a little darker than the surface, like GitHub's subtle canvas. A hairline in a grey between the grid's and the baseline's runs along its inner edge and under its header. The cursor's row is the card's highlight mixed onto that ground, a little stronger while the panel has the keys (on dark, 8 % toward strong with them and 5.5 % without). There's no accent bar.
1475. **For veto:** where it sits. At the figure's right, about 31 % of the width and 260 to 420 px; in an area under 560 px wide, the bottom 40 %, with smaller margins so a 240 px area still shows two rows. Its title shares the chart title's top padding, so the two baselines line up, and its kind line lines up with the subtitle; a panel under 140 px tall drops the kind line. Its sides are padded 20 px, rows are 22 px, and headings 34 px with the space above them, in 10.5 px semibold capitals tracked 0.8 px and drawn a character at a time. Beside a panel at the right, the figure keeps the whole area's side padding, at most 6 % of its own width, so nothing moves as the panel opens; the look reviewer found the title and the plot moving 8.6 px left. Over a panel at the bottom, a chart gives way by its legend, axis titles and title before its plot gets short (1506).
1476. **For veto:** rows. A row that names a mark (a series' value, a neighbour, an edge's end) follows m3: a swatch, the name in the text colour, the value at the right. Data values are 12 px tabular in the text colour; notes such as `6 edges` and `no data` are 11 px and muted. The key column is measured over every row, folded or not, so folding never moves the values: at least 64 px, at most 45 % of the inner width, from the four longest keys at each indent. Fold triangles are drawn paths, muted, pointing right when folded and down when open, and the 14 px gutter for them is kept only in a section that has a branch, as in m3's METADATA, so plain fields stay flush left. A heading never shows alone at the foot of the body, `reveal` brings a heading into view above the cursor's row when there's room, and the scrollbar is a 3 px muted thumb like the log's. JSON shows at most 500 members of an object or an array, then a muted `+N more`, and nests at most 12 deep. A missing value says `no data` and a JSON null `null`, both muted.
1477. **For veto:** a node's tree is titled by its label, else its id, and its kind line is `group · degree N` (`node · degree N` without a group). Its rows: id, label (left out where the label is the id), group, degree (`5`, or `5 · 2 in, 1 out` when directed), the scalar fields, and nested fields as folded branches. Then NEIGHBOURS, each neighbour once and in edge order, as m3 lists worker's, noted `N edges` where there are several, `y` copying its id; then METADATA from `metadata`, scalars first and nested values folded; and FIG, the node's fig block, last. A table's node drops a name or title column that equals its label, and its parent row shows the parent's label and names that node: hovering it lights the node, `⏎` goes there, and `y` copies the raw id. Two reviewers found a table's node saying its name twice and its parent by raw id.
1478. **For veto:** an edge's tree is titled `a → b` when directed and `a — b` when not, its kind line `directed edge · label` or `edge`. Its rows: label, weight and the scalar fields, then ENDS naming both nodes, then METADATA and FIG. A double click on an edge opens it (1493).
1479. **For veto:** a chart point's tree is titled with the card's header (`10:25 · Sat 21 Nov`), its kind line `series · point i of n`. Its rows: the x in full (`2026-11-21 10:25`); VALUES, each visible series at that x, picked as the card picks them but in legend order rather than the card's high to low, so `j` and `k` stay predictable, each naming its sample; a `total` under a hairline when two or more are stacked, semibold in the values' column; and ROW, the row's other columns, leaving out the x, y, colour, y2 and size fields, the fields a fold took apart, and fig. That heading reads `Row N` when no transform moved the rows, else `Row`. A scatter's point is titled with its series' name and lists x, y and size. Hidden series are left out.
1480. **For veto:** a series' tree: count; min, max and last, each written `v at x` and naming its sample, so `⏎` jumps there; the mean to one decimal past the series' precision with a trailing zero trimmed (`778`, not `778.0`); and FIG, what its block sets. It opens from `⏎` or a double click on the panel's row of the value the cursor is on already, or on a row naming a series; the figure's cursor stays and the panel keeps the keys. The correctness reviewer found the series' tree out of the editor's reach.
1481. **For veto:** a band's tree has the x axis's name with the category, then VALUES, a row for each series' part, each naming it, with the kind line `bar · 2 series`. A stacked band ends with its total; a grouped band, its bars side by side, has none, which the look reviewer found meaningless there. A cell's tree is titled `x · y`, with rows for x, y, the value under the colour field's name, and ROW.
1482. **For veto:** the panel's keys. `j` and `k` skip headings. `h` on a member goes up to its branch, and on an open branch folds it; `l` on a folded branch unfolds it, and on an open one goes to its first member. `⏎` folds or unfolds a branch, does nothing on a plain field, and on a row that names a mark takes the figure's cursor there. `gg` and `G` go to the first and last row. A fold that hides the cursor moves it to the branch. `y` copies the value as shown, except that a branch copies its compact JSON, a multi-line string its original text, and a neighbour or an end its id; `Y` copies `key: value`.
1483. **For veto:** the hint at the panel's foot, in the error figure's key-cap style: `Tab panel · ⏎ close` without the keys; with them `j k move`, then `⏎ go to` where the row names a mark, `h l fold` where the tree has branches, and `y copy`. Groups drop from the end when it's narrow, and it shows only when the body has room for 8 rows.
1484. **For veto:** search ranks with fig's own score (`algo::search::score`): exact, then a prefix (the shorter first), then a substring (the earlier first), then a subsequence (fewer bytes skipped first), ties by the label's byte order, equal labels in the order given. An empty query matches everything. A new query makes the best match current for `n`, and `next` and `prev` wrap.
1485. **For veto:** a band chart's table has every category as a row, empty ones too, and a heatmap's is a grid with the y axis's name in the corner, a row for each y category and a column for each x category. `tsv_at` existed from phase 2 and put the cursor on its row; the surface now calls it (1498).
1486. Deviations in the panel. Files it doesn't own, each changed as little as it could, all additions: scene.rs's `Hit::DetailFold(u32)`; lib.rs's `pub mod search;` and its line in the module docs; plot/mod.rs's `pub mod detail;` and a new public field on the pinned `ChartModel`, `drawn`, each layer's table after its transforms, which ROW reads. `panel` takes `state: PanelState` by value, and `keys` takes `(&mut DetailTree, &mut PanelState, PanelKey)`, the key an enum with `PanelKey::from_name` for keymaps; `reveal`, `scroll` and `toggle` are extra, and the surface calls `reveal` after a move or a fold and when it opens the panel, as the error log's reveal works. `Item::Edge`'s `cursor()` is None, because `Cursor` has no edge variant, and the surface picks the nearer end; `ChartModel::detail` returns None for `Cursor::Node`. A graph's trees come from `node_tree` and `edge_tree` over plain facts (`NodeFacts`, `EdgeFacts`, `Neighbour`), which the surface's glue in graph/detail.rs fills from `GraphModel`. The search model gives the ranking, the matched ranges and `n` `N`; how the prompt meets it was the surface's to decide (1497). With no graph to render beside, the builder compared the panel with m3's for m3's worker and for an edge beside a blank figure, from a demo kept in the run's scratchpad and never committed. At 400 × 240 its render of many.json beside the panel had overlapping ticks, which 1506 fixed.

**die-fig phase 3: graphs and the panel in the editor (P3-surface, 2026-11-22)**

1487. The surface. `\v` draws fig's graph JSON, a grammar graph spec and a CSV with id and parent columns as it does a chart, and a `.fig` holding a graph opens as its figure (1421). The layout runs in the build's `ctx.spawn` job, and the last frame stays up until it lands. Graphs no longer say they arrive in a later build (1416); tiles, dashboards and command sources still do. A graph has the chart's keys where they mean something (1466), and mouse-ux §4.5's pointer: a node, then an edge, under hover; focus after the dwell; a click puts the cursor on a node or an edge's nearer end, and on the background does nothing; ⇧-click sets the reference node and a path shows; a double click on a node or an edge opens its detail, and on the background fits; a node dragged and pinned is one undo step, and `Esc` mid-drag puts it back; a drag on the background, ⌥-drag or a middle drag pans 1:1; the wheel pans both ways and ⌘-wheel zooms about the pointer; and every rule of mouse-ux §3 holds as on charts. The panel opens on charts and graphs alike.
1488. **For veto:** `⏎` on a figure opens the detail panel for what the cursor is on, and `⏎` again closes it; the cursor's values in the messages (1354) are gone. With no cursor it's an error, `no cursor yet`, rather than a message.
1489. **For veto:** `Esc` clears the first of these there is: the errors' log, a gesture under way (a brush dropped, a pan taken back, a node's drag eased back), the key card, the search's emphasis, the detail panel (with the keys or not), the reference, and on a graph the cursor's emphasis (1459). That extends 1360. Mouse-ux §8 put the reference before the panel; the panel goes first, as the newest transient.
1490. **For veto:** `Tab` and `S-Tab` each move the keys between the figure and the panel, two stops for now (ruling 7), with `<C-i>` bound as `Tab`. `Tab` with no panel says `no detail panel: ⏎ opens one for the cursor`. The arrow keys are bound to the `h` `j` `k` `l` motions on every figure, in the figure and the panel alike.
1491. **For veto:** `y` and `Y` are figure keys (`fig-yank`, `fig-yank-pair`) that act only while the panel has the keys, and otherwise say how to get there. A yank goes through `Fig::copying_with(kill)`, and your config passes `die::vim::kill_new`, so `y` copies to the clipboard and the kill ring as Evil's `y` does; without that hook a yank sets the unnamed register and the clipboard. Each yank says `Copied …`. That's one edit to die-dev's src/lib.rs, so die-fig reaches die-vim's kill ring without depending on die-vim (D14); die-dev is 317 lines, from 314. die-dev's tests/fig.rs gains a graph round trip through your config: a hover, a click, the panel, a yank and back.
1492. **For veto:** the panel under the pointer (mouse-ux §4.10). A click anywhere on the panel gives it the keys, and a click on a row moves its cursor there; a click in the figure takes the keys back. A row that names a mark brings it forward in the figure while the pointer is on it, and that emphasis goes with its row when a key rebuilds the panel. A fold triangle folds on a click, and a double click on it folds once rather than folding and unfolding. A double click on a row that names a mark takes the figure's cursor there, as `⏎` does, and on a branch folds it. The wheel scrolls the panel. The interaction reviewer's major was a click in the figure leaving the keys with the panel, so the next key acted on the panel; its minors were a row's emphasis outliving the row and the triangle's double click.
1493. **For veto:** the panel follows the cursor, rebuilding for the cursor's mark whenever it moves, by a key, a click, a search or a jump. When the data or the hidden set changes, it rebuilds for its own mark and keeps its row and scroll, and it closes when its mark is gone. If the cursor lands on something with no detail, it keeps what it showed. A double click on an edge opens the edge's detail: the first click has already put the cursor on the nearer end, and the panel keeps the edge until the cursor moves.
1494. The panel is drawn on the base beside the figure. When only the panel changes, its rectangle redraws and presents alone, so moving through its rows never redraws the graph or the chart.
1495. **For veto:** on a chart, the keyboard cursor's card hides while the panel is open, since the panel shows the same values; a hover's card stays. The look reviewer found both up at once.
1496. **For veto:** search as you type. On a graph every node the query matches stays lit and the rest dim, the best match is in focus with the cursor on it, and its card shows; after `⏎` only the find stays emphasised. On a chart every matching series and category stays bright while you type, and a category's find brings its band's bars forward until `Esc`. What's hidden (a series, a graph's group) isn't searched, a find never shows a hidden group again, and `n` `N` skip what's hidden after the search. Typing the query back to nothing, or to one that matches nothing, puts the cursor and the view back, with the prompt still open. The characters matched aren't marked in the labels (1508). The review found a chart's search lighting only the best match and a category's find emphasising nothing (two reviewers), the cursor put on a hidden group's node or a hidden series, and an emptied query leaving the cursor, emphasised, on the last match.
1497. **For veto:** the `/` prompt lists its matches in fig's order (1484: the tier and its tie-break, then byte order), where phase 2's kept flex's order within each tier, so its first row is the match the cursor is on, and `⏎` takes the row chosen, the best unless you moved. `n` and `N` go on with what you typed, not the chosen row's label, and visit each node once however many of its names (its label and its id) match, round the ends. The interaction reviewer's blocker was `n` `N` cycling a few matches and never reaching the rest when a node's label differed from its id, and two reviewers found `⏎` moving the cursor off the match it had jumped to as you typed.
1498. **For veto:** `t` on a chart opens its table with the cursor on the cursor's row (`tsv_at`, which settles 1335). On a graph it opens one buffer, `*fig table: NAME*`, with the node table, a blank line and the edge table, each with its header, the cursor on the cursor's node: contract-p3 asked for two buffers or one with two sections, and said to say which.
1499. **For veto:** `s` on a graph offers `labels: on/off`, `layout: force/tree/layered` with the one drawn marked `(now)`, and `relayout: let every pin go`, which eases the pins back. Each is one undo step. A layout switch clears the window and the pins, since the old layout's units mean nothing in the new one, and its undo or redo puts back the view it saved (the pins and the window) once the relaid graph lands, not before. Picking the layout drawn says `settings: layout tree already` and makes no undo step, and picking `tree` keeps the spec's root. The review found undo of a layout switch losing its pins and its zoom (two reviewers), and `tree (now)` dropping the root and flipping the tree.
1500. **For veto:** `x`, `o` and `X` on a graph act on the cursor's node's group. When that node has no group, `x` and `o` say `the cursor's node has no group`, and ⌥-click on such a node says `fig: the node has no group`. `]` or `[` with no cursor says `fig: no cursor yet: h l j k or a click puts it on the data`. The interaction reviewer found keys and clicks on a graph doing nothing silently where their siblings said why.
1501. **For veto:** while a node is dragged it's the node in focus, its neighbours and edges bright as they follow it, and the cursor's card hides, as it does during a pan. `Esc` mid-drag eases the node back over `MOTION` (180 ms); the interaction reviewer found it snapping back.
1502. **For veto:** following an edit keeps the view and the pins of every node whose id survives. While the spec says `auto` and no setting picks a layout, following keeps the layout `auto` first picked, so an edit that makes a tree cyclic doesn't lay it out anew; a restart picks again. An edit that turns a graph into a chart (a table's `parent` column renamed) charts it fresh, letting the graph's window, pins and cursor go. The correctness reviewer's majors were an edit dropping the pins and the view when auto's pick changed, though every id survived, and an edited graph's window, in layout units, kept as the chart's domains.
1503. **For veto:** the status line on a graph reads `NAME  N nodes  M edges`, then `K groups hidden` and `P pinned` where there are any. `?` on a graph shows a graph's key card of 19 rows, and the chart's row for `⏎` reads `⏎ Tab  detail panel, into it and back`.
1504. **For veto:** the session keeps a graph's pins, the layout and labels `s` chose, and whether the panel was open, beside phase 2's view (1351). After a restart the panel opens again for the kept cursor once the graph is built.
1505. **For veto:** the three carried fixes. `\v` from a figure made by `:fig rates.fig` goes to the file's text, not the buffer `:fig` was typed in, and stays there (1426). Value ticks in a short plot drop from the bottom up, so the lowest, usually the baseline's 0, is kept (1432). The mismatch glyph is scaled to 82 % about its centre, so it stays as far inside its disc as the document and axes glyphs do.
1506. **For veto:** a short chart gives way further. Past its end labels, the ladder drops the legend, then the axis titles, then the title, keeping a plot of at least 48 px or half the height, and colliding x ticks are thinned to every other or every third rather than cut to one. The look reviewer found the panel at the bottom of a 400 × 240 area leaving the chart a 10 px plot, and the correctness reviewer, checking 1432's fix, found many.json at 200 × 120 with a 1 px plot and its `Hour` title cut at the bottom, which phase 3 didn't cause.
1507. Deviations in the surface. Files it doesn't own, each changed as little as it could: graph/overlay.rs (`OverlayState.matches` and one emphasis branch), graph/mod.rs (`pub mod detail;`), the new graph/detail.rs (the glue, one `impl GraphModel`), plot/layout.rs (the ticks' drop), plot/errors.rs (the glyph), two tests in tests/plot.rs, and fig_shot (the merge, `--detail` for graphs with the panel laid out as the surface lays it, and `--search Q`). In types it owns: `Gesture::NodeDrag`'s `before` is the whole `View`, since a drag pins and holds the window and undo and `Esc` need both back; `Toggles` gains `layout: Option<LayoutPick>`, a new Copy enum in interact/view.rs since `spec::Layout` isn't Copy, with `apply_graph`, `graph_layout`, `graph_labels` and `flip_graph_labels`, a graph's labels reusing `Toggles.labels`; `FigState::detail(w)`, the old message, is gone, and `Act::put_cursor` is new for the panel's jumps; on a graph `pane.hover` holds the hovered node as `Cursor::Node`, None on an edge, with the whole target in a new `pane.target`. The surface's code for graphs and the panel is in surface/graph.rs and surface/panel.rs.
1508. Not built: the characters a search matched marked in a graph's labels; mouse-ux §10's eased layout switch, so a switch relays out and fits at once; a fade for the graph's dwell emphasis; and §15's tree collapse, hops arc and box select, which it lists as later. using-die.md's What isn't there yet has them.
1509. The fixer's deviations. It changed files outside P3-surface's list: graph/{label, overlay, geom, draw, detail, mod}.rs, algo/layered.rs, plot/{overlay, layout, detail, mod}.rs, spec/fig.rs, interact/view.rs, fig_shot and tests/{graph, algo, plot, detail, spec}.rs. Its new public API is all additions, with the pinned `plot::base`, `plot::overlay` and `graph::base` unchanged: `plot::base_beside`, `graph::base_beside`, `plot::overlay::{Lit, overlay_lit}`, `GraphModel::path_shown`, `Toggles::graph_layout_for`, `graph::OverlayState.calm`, `graph::label::OWN_MARGIN`, `NodeLabel.ring`, `source::Job.auto`, an `auto` argument on `source::graph_model`, and the pane's `found_band`, `lit`, `lit_bands` and `calm` with `Pane::chart_lit`. Three tests changed: tests/algo.rs's exact positions (1448), graph_surface's `Esc` mid-drag test, which now waits for the ease to settle (1501), and tests/spec.rs's duplicate id message (1470).

**die-fig phase 3: the review (2026-11-22)**

1510. Three reviewers, each with probes of its own: the look, from fig_shot's renders; correctness; and the interaction, against mouse-ux. 42 findings, 38 distinct since four were found twice: 1 blocker, 15 majors and 22 minors. The blocker was the interaction reviewer's: `n` `N` cycling a few matches (1497). The look reviewer's six majors were the overlay's labels reading as other nodes' (1455); same-rank edges through the nodes between (1449); a small area's card over the neighbours, the title and the legend, and a card straying from what it's about (1458); a grouped band's total (1481); and the bottom dock's 10 px plot (1506). Its ten minors were dimmed labels cut by the card and a hovered edge's card over the edge (1458), corner labels and far rings and two labels run together (1454), hubs losing their labels at 400 × 240 while leaves keep theirs, the self-loop (1452), the cursor's card beside the panel (1495), the figure moving 8.6 px as the panel opens (1475), a table node's tree (1477) and a tree in layers with its parents off centre (1448). The correctness reviewer's five majors were `⏎` moving the cursor off the find (1497), undo of a layout switch (1499), an edit changing auto's pick and an edited graph's window kept by a chart (1502), and the hover's 4.1 ms (1471); its seven minors were `tree (now)` (1499), a chart's search lighting only the best match (1496), paths through hidden nodes (1469), `{"nodes": []}` and the duplicate id's message (1470), a table node's tree (1477), the series' tree out of reach (1480), and many.json at 200 × 120 (1506), which came before phase 3. The interaction reviewer's six majors beside its blocker were `⏎` in `/` (1497), undo of a layout switch (1499), a click in the figure leaving the keys with the panel (1492), the graph staying dimmed (1459), search putting the cursor on what isn't drawn and a chart's search lighting only the best match (1496); its seven minors were a row's emphasis outliving the row and the triangle's double click (1492), `Esc` mid-drag snapping and the dwell's missing fade (1501, 1460), a node dragged under the panel (1465), silent keys and clicks (1500), an emptied query (1496), and the guide's What isn't there yet still saying graphs and the panel weren't built (fe4af83). All are fixed, the code's with tests, but these, in whole or in part, for veto: hubs at 400 × 240 still lose their labels, since their crowded neighbours block every spot though hubs are placed first, and What isn't there yet says so; the dwell's emphasis still has no fade (1460); the hover's raster is over 2 ms, accepted against 8 (1471); and a click on the background still doesn't let the cursor's emphasis go, only `Esc` does, since contract-p3 §4 has a background click do nothing (1459).

Through your config it opens services.json and `\v`, its nodes in their groups' colours once the layout's job lands; a hover's dwell brings worker forward, the rest under the wash and its card up; a click; `h` `j` `k` `l` each going that way and kept in sight; mailer dragged to an empty place and pinned with nothing else moving; `u` and `<C-r>`, so a pin is still there for the later edit; a background pan, 1:1; a ⌘-wheel zoom keeping db under the pointer; the panel beside the graph on its own ground, `Tab` into it and `j` presenting its rectangle alone; `/` lighting the matches with the panel following the find, and `Esc` back; `t`'s two tables on worker's row; `0` fitting the pin in before a split, as you would; an edit of the JSON in the split with vim keys (`gg/"mailer<CR>/platform<CR>ciwdata<Esc>`) moving mailer from platform to data, which the graph follows once, as a colour change in the base's pixels as well as in the model, with the pin and every place kept; and `\v` back with the figure gone. Hiding a dimmed node under a label's ground costs 6 edges' worth, from 1, so a label crosses two bright edges before a node goes from the picture.

And the panel shows whole rows only, so worker's panel leaves about 20 px above the key hints, with db and metrics below the fold and the scrollbar saying so.
