**die-fig phase 4: what it is and how it was built (2026-11-22)**

1529. Phase 4 is the design study's fourth: "Dashboard rows; stat, meter and sparkline; the linked crosshair and zoom; the time range; the run, every and stream sources; follow mode; heatmaps." It also takes in your `.fig` format of 2026-11-22 (rows of panels, a panel that is another `.fig`, named sources and `x.fig#name` borrows) and an error figure for each panel (2b's "Dashboards"). There's still no dial (1194), and the study's m4, a dashboard of four stat tiles over two charts that share a crosshair, was the target for dashboards and tiles. A `.fig` holding a dashboard opens as one, as `\v` on a dashboard spec and `:fig x.fig` do: a header with the title, the time-range chip and the live status, then rows of rounded panels by `span` and `height`, each a chart, a graph, a heatmap, a tile or another `.fig`, scrolling when they don't fit. A `stat`, a `meter` and the new `sparkline` draw as m4's tiles, and one alone opens as a dashboard of that tile. Heatmaps gain a colour legend, a cell's hover and card, `h` `j` `k` `l` by cell and a brush that snaps to whole cells. The panels whose x is the dashboard's `time` field share one time: one moment under the pointer, one zoom, pan and brush, and `T`'s ranges. `run`, `every` and `stream` sources run their commands once you say yes, each in a process group of its own, and a time chart follows the newest rows. Dashboards, tiles and commands no longer "arrive in a later build" (1416); of the study's five phases, phase 5, the braille fallback and the finish, is what's left.
1530. How it was built. Three builders ran in parallel on code with no editor in it: the tiles and heatmaps (P4-tiles, fig/tiles), dashboards and the `.fig` layout (P4-dash, fig/dash), and commands as sources with the trust store's model and follow mode (P4-live, fig/live). Until the merge, P4-dash drew tiles through a placeholder with the tiles' pinned signatures. The surface's builder (P4-surface, fig/p4-surface) merged all three first: fig/tiles at a34f508; fig/dash at ce14b4a, whose conflicts in fig_shot, figure/mod.rs, spec/dash.rs and spec/write.rs the tiles builder had foreseen with a read-only `git merge-tree` and said how to resolve; and fig/live at e945b92, resolving source.rs by hand. Then it put them in the editor (a0dea1f, 51754c3, bf5a7ce, 6e40bc4). Three reviewers followed, each with probes of its own: the look, from renders of the fixtures; correctness; and the interaction, against mouse-ux. A fixer took their findings in two halves, the model's and the look's (985d115) and the editor's (7b55e74), then more tests (7ac1cbc). The lander merged fig/p4-surface into `m0/integration` at d1ca05d and added die-dev's end-to-end test (0f2da9a). A look pass on the merged tree came last, on fig/look-4 (152095d, 1850334), and `m0/integration` took it. `main` is still at phase 3's merge, 12694f9. docs/using-die.md's "## fig" covers dashboards, tiles, `T`, `f`, `R`, `G`, the commands and their questions, and the session, and its What isn't there yet says what's left.

**die-fig phase 4: tiles and heatmaps (P4-tiles, 2026-11-22)**

1531. The tiles. `figure::TileModel` is built for a stat, a meter or a sparkline over a table and a range, and `figure::base`, `overlay`, `hit` and `detail` draw it, hover it and give its tree, as contract-p4 §2.4 pinned them (1549). A stat aggregates its field (count, sum, mean, min, max, p50, p95 or `last`) over the range, compares it with the range before or with a number, and draws as m4's tile: the label, the value and its unit, the delta line with its arrow, and a trend with its newest dot. A meter is a track filled to `value / max`, coloured by the highest threshold it has passed. A sparkline (spec/tile.rs, a new `Figure::Sparkline`) is a stat's trend drawn larger, with a wash under it. Each has a card. The fixtures in examples/data/tiles/ are smooth walks, a row a minute in service.csv, with a spec for each of m4's four tiles, a meter at each status and a sparkline; examples/data/heatmap.json is requests by hour and weekday, 24 × 7, and heatmap-diverging.json a change from last week either way. fig_shot draws a row of tiles, and with `--brushed` a released brush's zoom.
1532. **For veto:** a stat's range holds the rows after its start up to and including its end, so the sample on the boundary counts in the period before and the two periods share none. The review's first blocker was a sum or a count that took the boundary sample twice, overstating the newest trend point. To keep m4's figures, the fixer retuned tiles/service.csv (the previous six hours scaled, `error_pct` up 0.0005, 06:00's sessions 3,342), so the tiles still read 1.28M, 0.41 %, 182 ms and 3,412, with 4.2 %, 0.08 pt, 12 ms and 2.1 %. Two tests now expect 360 rows rather than 361, a mean of 280.5, `128 %` and a delta of 1.08.
1533. **For veto:** a stat's trend is its range in twelve parts, each part's aggregate a point; with twelve distinct times or fewer, each time is a point. The parts are whole numbers of the data's sampling step laid back from the newest sample, and a sum's or a count's partial oldest part is dropped, so a sum over flat data is flat at any range; data with no steady spacing keeps twelve equal parts of time. The look reviewer found sum and count trends zigzagging on any range that didn't divide into twelve equal counts. `last` is the newest row by the trend's field, else by the table's first time column.
1534. **For veto:** the delta's words. A change is relative, `▲ 4.2 % vs previous 6h`, with one decimal under 10 % and `< 0.1 %` under 0.05 %; with a `%` unit it's in points (`0.08 pt`), and with another unit in that unit (`12 ms`). Against a fixed number it says `vs 808 ms`. The card says `+4.2 %  change, good`. With no rows before the range, the line says `no data for previous …` in muted text. A row of stats says as much of its period as its most cramped tile can (1633).
1535. **For veto:** what a card says a stat aggregates: `sum of rpm`, `mean of`, `median of`, `p95 of`, `count of rows`, `highest x`, `lowest x`, `newest x`.
1536. **For veto:** stat, meter and sparkline specs gain a `unit` key, since m4 shows units and the specs had no way to say them. The look reviewer found health.fig, the m4 fixture, with none, so it missed m4's `%`, `ms` and `pt`; its error rate and p95 have their units now. Its own numbers (526K, 0.077 pt, 6.55 ms) still differ from m4's, which only the tiles' fixture reproduces (1571).
1537. **For veto:** a stat is m4's tile at m4's size, 197 × 100, but for its unit, 6 px after the value rather than m4's 4: die-draw's figures sit closer to their advance, so 6 reads as m4's gap. Its content hangs from the top as m4's does, the label's baseline 26 px down whatever else the tile has, and a tile too short for that centres its label and value. A row's tiles share one value size, the smallest any of them needs, through a new pub field, `TileModel::fit` (a `RowFit`), which the dashboard and fig_shot's tile rows set. The look reviewer found each tile centring whatever it had, so the labels across a row didn't line up.
1538. **For veto:** a stat gives way as contract-p4 §2.1 says: the trend first, then the delta (its period, then the rest), then the unit. The value never ellipsizes. It shrinks to 18 px to keep its unit, and in a short tile its label, before either goes, and once it has lost its unit it stays at 18 px rather than growing again as the tile narrows. The look reviewer found the unit going before the delta, and the label going before the value shrank.
1539. **For veto:** a meter's status is an icon and a lower-case word, `⚠ warning`, left of the share, and its threshold ticks are hairlines under the fill, showing above and below it. As it narrows, `of max` goes first, then the word, each kept only while the label has room for its whole width, or 96 px of a longer one; then the label is cut, and the icon never goes. A row's meters drop their words all at once: each shows its word only if every meter in the row fits its own with its label whole, where the look reviewer found the word shown for good and serious but not for warning and critical at m4's width. A meter too narrow for its label, icon and share on one line puts the label on a line of its own, with the icon and the share under it. The track stays the grid colour, as contract-p4 has it, though the dataviz rules would have a lighter step of the fill's ramp. A meter's natural height is 64 px, and a stat's and a sparkline's 100 px. The look pass changed `of max` on a meter with no thresholds (1630).
1540. **For veto:** a sparkline's line, wash and dot are in series slot 1, where a stat's trend is grey. From 84 px tall, a tile row's height, its label and value go over the line, and a tall sparkline's card goes beside its crosshair at the line's top. The look pass changed where the label and the card go when they don't fit (1631, 1632).
1541. **For veto:** a heatmap's sequential ramp is the dataviz palette's blue, steps 150 to 650 on light (#b7d3f6 to #104281) and 650 to 150 on dark, six steps 0.095 apart in OKLCH lightness and mixed in OKLab between them, so the least value recedes toward the surface on either, at 1.5:1 against it on light and 1.8:1 on dark. `validate_palette.js --ordinal` passes its monotone lightness, its step gaps and its single hue against #fcfcfb and #1a1a1a, and fails its 2:1 floor for the end nearest the surface, which palette.md waives for a sequential ramp. 250 to 650 and 550 to 150 passed every check, but the night hours read loud.
1542. **For veto:** a diverging scale (`"scale": {"type": "diverging", "mid": 0}`) is red below the midpoint and blue above, as RdBu has it, three steps an arm from a grey midpoint (#f0efec light, #383835 dark): 250, 450 and 650 on light, 550, 350 and 150 on dark, reaching as far either side as the data's farther end, so equal distances take equal colours. palette.md documents no red ramp, so the reds are derived at the blue steps' OKLCH lightness at hue 25°; each arm passes `validate_palette --ordinal`, but the reds aren't documented palette colours. A heatmap's colours come only from these ramps, and a series' fig colour is ignored.
1543. **For veto:** heatmap cells. A band axis has no padding, and each cell sits 1 px inside its slot, snapped to physical pixels, which leaves the 2 px surface gap. A cell with no value draws hollow, a grid-grey outline, so a gap never reads as the least value. A heatmap's cells may grow until one fills the plot, a zoom floor of one cell on band axes, since the bars' 30 px band rule had stopped x zoom altogether on a 24-column heatmap at 880 px; that holds for the wheel and the keys too.
1544. **For veto:** the hovered cell's ring and the cursor's are 2 px in the strong text colour outside a 2 px band of the surface's, reaching 4 px past the cell over its neighbours' edges, so they read on every step; contract-p4 asked for 2 px in the text colour, which the look reviewer found nearly invisible on the hottest cells. A test holds every ramp step at 3:1 or more against the ring or the band. The keyboard cursor shows chips for both categories, the hovered cell brightens its row's and its column's labels, and the cursor's ring stays while the pointer hovers another cell.
1545. **For veto:** a cell's card. Its header gives a bare-number category its axis's name (`Hour 10 · Thu`), and the row's other fields from JSON are formatted as values (`1,191`). It keeps off its cell, the title, the colour legend and the axes' titles where there's room, covering other cells instead. Its swatch, where it's under 1.6:1 against the card's ground, gets a hairline ring in the text colour at 45 %; the look reviewer found a near-midpoint cell's swatch disappearing on dark.
1546. **For veto:** the colour legend, a 160 × 8 px ramp with the least and the most as ticks, the midpoint on a diverging scale, and the field's name, goes under the title with `legend: top`, in a column at the right with `legend: right`, and nowhere with `legend: none`. At small sizes it now outlasts the axes' titles, where the look reviewer found it dropped before them. Its scale spans all the data, so a zoom never recolours.
1547. **For veto:** the box brush on a heatmap snaps to whole cells on both axes and zooms to them, its readout `7 – 15 · Tue – Sat`. A y chip hides only the tick labels it overlaps, with a 1.5 px allowance, so a heatmap's neighbouring rows keep theirs; it used to clear 4 px above and below.
1548. The tile fixtures are dated 2026-11-21, a Saturday, so the cards say `Sat 21 Nov` where m4 says `Fri`.
1549. Deviations in the tiles. `Point` in `hit` is `(f32, f32)`, since the crate has no Point type, and `TileHover` is `Tile { at }` or `Trend { point, card }`. Pub items beyond §2.4: `TileModel::build_in` (an explicit zone, where `build` uses the system's), `overlay_in` (which keeps the card inside the window's area), `hover_at(model, t)` (a linked tile's mini crosshair) and `draw::hit_with` (with the fonts' measure, where `hit` uses FixedMeasure); the fixer added `TileModel::build_on` and `fit` (1567, 1537). `AggOp`, a pinned enum, gains `Last`, which data/transform.rs's reduce handles. It made the smallest edits it could in files it doesn't own: spec/dash.rs (`unit` on stats and meters, a meter's `thresholds`, their key lists and docs), spec/mod.rs (`StatSpec.unit`, `MeterSpec.unit` and `thresholds`, `Threshold`, `Channel.mid`, `sparkline` in the type lists), spec/write.rs (writing those and the sparkline), spec/chart.rs (the diverging scale, `last` among the aggregates), source.rs (a sparkline's `kind_plural`) and three expected messages in tests/spec.rs that now list `sparkline` and `last`; plot/detail.rs's `other_fields` counts as heatmap work. fig_shot's tile renders draw a dashboard-like frame and margin of their own, since `figure::base` draws only inside its rectangle.

**die-fig phase 4: dashboards and the .fig layout (P4-dash, 2026-11-22)**

1550. Dashboards. `dashboard::DashModel::build` builds every panel's model, each with its own result: a chart, a graph, a tile, a nested `.fig`, waiting for its data or a yes, or its errors, so one bad panel shows its error figure and the rest draw. Sources are read once and shared. `dashboard::layout` and `layout_full` lay out m4's header and the rows, each panel's figure by its own layout in its frame. `base` and `overlay` draw the frames, the focus ring, each figure in place, a failed panel's error figure and the stale badge, the hovered panel's own overlay, and in the others the linked moment, a shared card and the linked brush; a hit names the panel and the panel's own hit. Fixtures in examples/data/dash/: health.fig (m4: four stat tiles over requests.csv and service.csv, then "Requests per minute" and "Errors per minute" side by side, `time` 6h), ops.fig (a graph beside a heatmap, a column of two charts, a meter row), nested.fig (a `file` panel, a nested dashboard and an `x.fig#name` borrow), broken-panel.fig (a missing file and a mismatch among good panels) and tall.fig (more rows than fit). fig_shot draws dashboards with flags for the focus, full size, a range or a custom time, the scroll, the live status, the pointer at a panel's time, a panel's cursor, a shared card and a panel's log.
1551. **For veto:** the `.fig` format as agreed (fig-format-notes.md). `rows` is a list of rows, each a list of panels or `{"height": 2, "panels": […]}`. A panel is any figure but a dashboard, `{"file": "x.fig", "span": 2}` (that whole figure, its paths from its own directory, a dashboard drawn as a nested group), or `{"column": […], "span": 1}`. A dashboard's `data` names sources, a panel's `"data": "req"` uses one, and `"data": "x.fig#name"` borrows one from another `.fig`. A dashboard's `"tooltip": "shared"` puts a card in every linked panel. File panels and borrows nest at most four deep, with or without a file of the dashboard's own (the correctness reviewer found five when it had none), and a cycle is a mismatch at the reference's place. Every row and panel keeps its place for errors, and spec/write.rs writes it all back in the same shape.
1552. **For veto:** rows are 16 px apart (m4's tiles end at 192 and its charts start at 208), and panels 12 px apart across a row and down a column. Under the last row is a 16 px margin, m4's gap above the mode line. The sides are 28 px, narrowing as 3.2 % of the width, from 28 to 12 px, on narrow areas. Contract-p4's "28 px margins, 12 px gaps" holds at the sides and between panels.
1553. **For veto:** when the rows don't fit, the header stays put and the rows scroll under it, clipped, with a grid-coloured hairline under the header once they have. The scrollbar is a 4 px thumb, muted at 55 % with no track, centred in the right margin.
1554. **For veto:** a row whose panels can't each have their least width wraps onto as few lines as fit, sharing them evenly. The least widths: charts and graphs 220 px, tiles their `min_size`, and every panel at least 120 px. At 400 px wide, health's four tiles go two and two and each chart gets a line of its own. Each line of figures takes at least 45 % of the room under the header, up to 320 px (1636), and a graph panel at least what its ranks need (1570); the dashboard scrolls rather than squeezing them.
1555. **For veto:** a panel's chart leaves out its automatic end labels, since the legend says the same and m4 shows none; an explicit `"labels": "ends"` keeps them (through a new `Insets.ends`).
1556. **For veto:** the charts directly on one row share their plot's top and bottom, so their grids line up across the row, as m4's do, though only where their ticks fall alike: at 880 × 470 the left chart's top gridline (1,000) still sits below the right chart's (30), since a y top isn't niced to a tick (1620).
1557. **For veto:** a panel's chart lays out 8 px narrower than its frame, so its last x tick label keeps about 12 px from the ring, and its plot ends 24 px in, where m4's ends 26 px in. It leaves 7 px more above its top tick than a chart alone, so the plot starts where m4's does, at 278 rather than 271; the look reviewer found the panels' charts tighter under their legends than m4's.
1558. **For veto:** a plot under 120 px tall whose nice top would pass 1.3 times the data's top ends at the data's top plus 4 %, which is then not a tick. The look reviewer found an 84 px plot at 400 px wide with a y axis niced to double its data.
1559. **For veto:** time ticks gain a two-hour step, which d3 doesn't have, so six hours read 06:00, 08:00, 10:00 and 12:00 as in m4, where the look reviewer found fewer; some twelve-hour widths go from three-hour steps to two. One scale test now asks for 4 ticks rather than 6 to keep its three-hour case, and a two-hour case was added.
1560. **For veto:** the linked moment in the other linked panels is a dashed hairline in the foreground at 40 %, as contract-p4 says, where m4's mockup draws a solid #444. Each series' dot there gets a small value tag like m4's `17`: the card's ground, a ring, 11 px semibold, right of the line, or left of it at the plot's edge. A tag that would overlap another, or another dot, is left out.
1561. **For veto:** with nothing hovered, the focused panel's keyboard cursor drives the linked moment, a tile's cursor on its trend too, where the interaction reviewer found a tile's cursor driving none. Only the focused panel shows its cursor's card; cursors in the other panels show without one.
1562. **For veto:** the hovered panel's title brightens to white on dark and black on light. Titles are already #eeeeee at rest, as in m4, so the change is small.
1563. **For veto:** the focused panel's ring is `Theme::other`, #5a5a5a on dark and #b5b4ad on light, never the accent.
1564. **For veto:** a `file` panel holding a dashboard draws as a group: a frame with 10 px corners in the grid colour, its title 13 px semibold 16 px in, and its rows laid out 12 px inside. A nested panel whose x is the outer dashboard's time field links to the outer time.
1565. **For veto:** a waiting panel draws its frame and title only, and one with no title says why it waits (`waiting for ./ticker.sh`) in 12 px muted where the title would be. A waiting or failed tile keeps its label as a tile draws it, 12 px muted, not as a 13 px panel title.
1566. **For veto:** a failed panel's error figure names the panel (`Can't draw Throughput`), else its own file, else `this panel`, never the dashboard's file, which isn't what failed; the data file stays in the message. The error figure's words want 230 px inside it rather than 260 (plot/errors.rs), so a third of an 880 px dashboard shows the kind, the first error and the hint rather than only the glyph and the title; only areas 246 to 276 px wide change. A panel's error figure gets no source text, so its log has no caret line for an error in the dashboard's own text unless the error carries its line (1639).
1567. **For veto:** a tile is computed over the visible time whenever its table has the dashboard's time field, not only a stat with a trend, and it counts its range along that field (`TileModel::build_on`), its trend still along its own; under `All time` it counts every row, not the linked panels' extent. The correctness reviewer's blocker was the range applied to the trend's field, else the table's first time column, never the dashboard's. Only a stat whose trend is the time field, or a sparkline whose x is, is linked and shows the mini crosshair.
1568. **For veto:** the live status in the header shortens when it would meet the chip or the title: the full text, then its first word (`live`, `error`), then the dot alone. With no chip it sits on the title's line. With no title, no time and no live status, the rows start at the margin.
1569. **For veto:** a dashboard with a `time` but no range opens on all the linked data, and the chip says `All time`. While a live source is still coming, the chip names the spec's range; the look reviewer found `All time` while a live dashboard waited, though its spec asked for 15m.
1570. **For veto:** a graph panel's body is placed so the graph's own paddings put its legend 16 px in, where a chart's sits under the same title and subtitle. A tree's or layers' ranks drop the label line's margin above and below where they'd overlap, and a graph panel is at least 104 px tall plus the plot its ranks need, so ops.fig at 400 px scrolls further. Graph labels try two rings further out before they're dropped, and a label crossing an edge counts it once, not once a segment, so ops.fig's `worker` sits on a ground over the api→db edge. The look reviewer found the nodes overlapping in a 180 px panel, and two of nine nodes unlabelled with room to spare.
1571. **For veto:** the dashboard fixtures' numbers are synthetic and differ from m4's illustrative ones (Requests reads 526K rather than 1.28M).
1572. Deviations in the dashboard. `DashModel::build` takes a fourth argument, `origin: &Origin { file, dir, zone }`: the spec's own file for cycles, its directory for trust and as the commands' working directory, and the zone for the charts. `layout` and `layout_full` take the view and the scale, since each panel's figure is laid out under its view at a scale as `plot::base` is, and `overlay` takes the model, since the chart and graph overlays need theirs; `base` is as pinned. `DashView.focused` is an `Option<usize>`, None when a dashboard opens, so nothing is ringed until `Tab` or a click. Sources are keyed by what they resolve to (`Want`, a `DataSpec` and a directory), not by name, since a name means different data in each nested file and two names can resolve to one file; `DashModel::wants()` lists what to read, each once. `dashboard::read` and `open` read data files and nested specs for fig_shot, the tests and the surface's job, and never run a command: those panels wait. The smallest changes it could in files it doesn't own: plot/layout.rs (`Insets` and a parameter of `lay_out`), plot/mod.rs (`base_inset` and the `DataRef::Borrow` arm), plot/errors.rs (1566's threshold), source.rs and spec/chart.rs (the borrow), and tests/spec.rs (the row and panel fields renamed). The `.fig` shapes' tests are in tests/dashboard.rs, since tests/spec.rs wasn't its file. fig_shot takes a dashboard's flags out of the arguments before the figure's, in one `if`, which kept the merge with fig/tiles small; the look reviewer found them swallowing `--range`, so tile renders couldn't take a visible time, and the fixer made them a dashboard's only.

**die-fig phase 4: commands as sources, trust and follow mode (P4-live, 2026-11-22)**

1573. Live sources, in a new live.rs. `{"run": cmd, "every": "30s"}` runs `sh -c cmd` in the spec's directory with stdin closed, when the figure opens, at each tick and on `R`, its stdout read as a file would be (CSV, TSV, JSON or JSONL by sniffing) up to 64 MB. `{"stream": cmd}` starts when the figure opens, and its lines grow a table as they come, with types inferred as a file's are, the newest 200,000 rows kept, folded in at most 30 times a second. Each command runs in a process group of its own on threads it owns, is killed by group and is always waited for. `live::Trust` keeps the yeses by spec and command, and follow mode is a pure function of the view, the newest x and whether it follows. A build waits for a command and is built from what it printed (`source::build_with`). Fixtures in examples/data/live/: ticker.sh (a CSV row every 200 ms: time, rpm, errors), errors.sh (a small CSV, then it exits), fail.sh (exits 3 with stderr) and live.fig (a stream panel, a run panel every 5 s and stats over the stream).
1574. **For veto:** a run's ticks fall on a fixed grid from when it started, as `ctx.every`'s do. `R` runs at once without moving the grid, so an `R` just before a tick can mean two runs close together, and after a long sleep the missed ticks are skipped to the next grid point. A tick while a run goes is skipped.
1575. **For veto:** when any command ends, whatever it left running in its process group gets SIGTERM, then SIGKILL a second later, so a run can't leave a background process behind. A process that escapes the group (`setsid`) holding a pipe keeps only a reader thread waiting, never a process of ours. A command that ignores SIGTERM is killed with SIGKILL a second after the stop, waited for on a thread of its own, so the main thread never blocks; `Stopped::wait` is there for DIE's quit.
1576. **For veto:** commands run with `/bin/sh -c`, as contract-p4 says, not with `$SHELL` as `:fig !cmd` does. With `PathFrom::Login` they get your login shell's PATH (`die_core::process::path()`, as `:fig !cmd` and compile have it), where contract-p4 said the environment as inherited. The editor always uses `PathFrom::Login`, in tests too (1625).
1577. **For veto:** a stream's lines that aren't rows (bad JSON, more fields than the header, a quote left open, a line over 1 MB, a scalar) are skipped and counted, and the first is kept as `line N of ./x.sh's output: …`. Only a header that can't be one (two columns of one name) fails the stream, as malformed, and stops the command.
1578. **For veto:** a stream's types are inferred as it goes with the file reader's rules (number, time, category up to 50, text). A type a row ruled out stays out after that row drops off the front. A column that stops being numbers or times part-way writes its earlier cells as their values (1.5, RFC 3339 in UTC), not as the command spelt them; categories keep their exact strings.
1579. **For veto:** a stream's rows keep no fig blocks and no raw JSON objects, where a file's keep both, so the detail panel shows a stream row's columns only. A JSON `fig` key is never a column.
1580. **For veto:** `R` on a stream that has ended keeps its rows and appends the new process's, its first line a header again, the columns matched by name, so the chart doesn't flash empty. `R` on a running stream does nothing.
1581. **For veto:** a stream that ends having printed only a header still folds once, as an empty table, so its figure says `nothing to draw: ./x.sh has no rows`, naming the command when no file was read.
1582. **For veto:** the failure texts. `./fail.sh exited with status 3` (or `was stopped by signal N`), its log ``ran `./fail.sh` in DIR: exit 3`` and then up to 20 `stderr: …` lines, each cut at 400 characters; `X printed more than 64 MB`, its log `stopped at 64 MB`; `X printed nothing`, the empty kind; and `couldn't run X: …`. A parse error in what a run printed has its place in the message (`line 3, column 5 of what X printed: …`) and no position of its own, so the log's caret never points into the spec, and the note `read what it printed as CSV`. A figure that drew before keeps its last good data with the stale badge.
1583. **For veto:** a figure whose command hasn't printed yet isn't failing, it's waiting: `waiting for ./ticker.sh`, the empty kind, noted `streamed in DIR` or `run every 30s in DIR`, and the surface shows it as a quiet frame rather than the error figure (1605).
1584. **For veto:** the trust store. A yes is kept by the spec's absolute path and the exact command in the session, so a restart doesn't ask again; a no lasts this session only and is never kept, and a command in a spec with no file is trusted for the session only. Spec paths compare after `.` and `..` are taken out, so another spelling through a symlink asks again. A `file` panel or an `x.fig#name` borrow inside the spec's directory needs no yes; one outside it, by `..` or by a link out, checked on disk with links followed, is asked about before it's read, and a no shows `not opened: you said no` until `R`. Two reviewers found a `file` panel reached through `..` opening with no question, and the correctness reviewer a borrow from outside read with no yes. `R` forgets the noes of that figure's own specs only (`Trust::ask_again_for`), with its spec-less answers and refusals; the correctness reviewer found it forgetting every figure's, so other dashboards asked again unprompted.
1585. **For veto:** two of a dashboard's sources naming one command in one directory (with another `every`, or another format) each run as a process of their own, one yes for both, since `LiveKey` gains `nth` while trust stays keyed by spec and command. The correctness reviewer found only the first filled and the other's panels waiting for ever.
1586. **For veto:** follow mode. The view follows exactly while the window's right edge is within a pixel of the newest sample or past it, with the newest in view. Any change by hand (a pan, a zoom, a brush, a key) that lands at the end keeps or resumes following, not only the pan contract-p4 names. A view fitted to the data (no x set) counts as following and grows with it, and `G` and a reset resume and keep the window's width. New data never moves a view that isn't following.
1587. **For veto:** as rows drop from the front, the cursor stays on its sample, found by its series and x (the same y among equal x) or by a band's or cell's category names; when that sample has gone, the cursor goes rather than moving to another.
1588. **For veto:** a stream's first rows fold at once, then at least 1/30 s apart by the caller's clock, and its end comes only after its last rows have folded.
1589. **For veto:** live.fig's ticker.sh counts its times on from when it started in 0.2 s steps, not by the wall clock, written in ISO with tenths so the stream infers a time column, and errors.sh prints the ten minutes ending at the current minute for api and worker. live.fig uses the shorthand rows (`[[…]]`), not `{height, panels}`.
1590. Deviations in live. The smallest changes outside its files: data/csv.rs gains `pub(crate) fields(line, delim)` and `sniff_line(line)`, so a stream's line splits in the CSV reader's own dialect, and lib.rs gains `pub mod live;`. source.rs has `build_with(job, live_tables)` rather than a new `Job` field, since `Job` is built with literal syntax in files others own, and `Built` and `Failed` gain `feeds`. Waiting is a flag on each feed (`Failed::waiting()`, `is_waiting()`) rather than a second list on `Failed`, which pushed it past clippy's `result_large_err` limit. `source::LATER_RUN` went with the later-build path. Runs report through `Live::poll` on the surface's timer rather than as `ctx.spawn` jobs, so runs and streams take one path, and the surface's harness tests wait a little in real time between `advance`s, since `run_until_idle` doesn't wait on a command. `LiveKey.spec` is an `Option<PathBuf>`, None for a spec with no file. The trust question's words are the dashboard's (`NeedsTrust::question`), and a lone chart's command uses `NeedsTrust::Command`.

**die-fig phase 4: dashboards in the editor (P4-surface, 2026-11-22)**

1591. The surface. A `.fig` holding a dashboard, `\v` on a dashboard spec and `:fig x.fig` open it at once as frames and titles, planned in a job; each data file is read in a job of its own, and the panels are built again in one coalesced job as each lands. Every panel is a pane of its own, driven as it would be alone (hover, a click, a brush, a pan, the wheel, the keys), its layout lent to it, and the linked charts share one time, one undo history and their eases. `Tab` and `S-Tab` go through the panels, `f` shows one full size, `T` picks the time range (as a click on the chip does), `R` runs the commands again (as a click on the live status does), `G` follows, `!` turns a failed panel over to its log and `?` lists the dashboard's keys. Commands are asked for by name through the prompt, run and streamed while a window shows the figure, polled only while one is due, and stopped at quit. `t` and export work on a dashboard. A stat, meter or sparkline alone opens as a dashboard of that tile, at its natural height at the top of the window.
1592. **For veto:** with no panel focused, the first figure key (`h` `j` `k` `l`, `+`, `x`, …) focuses the first panel that draws, in reading order, rather than refusing; `f`, `⏎`, `t` and `s` say `Tab or a click focuses a panel first`.
1593. **For veto:** a press on a panel focuses it at the press, so a brush focuses its panel too, and on a title it only focuses. A click on a tile puts its cursor on the trend's newest point, `h` and `l` move it along the trend, and the focused tile shows its card there while the pointer is elsewhere. A double click on a linked plot, like `0` on a linked panel, puts the shared time back to `time.range`, not to all the data; on a title or a tile it shows the panel full size and back.
1594. **For veto:** the header's live status says `live · updated 12 s ago` at rest, `updating…` while a run goes, `error · 2 m ago` after a failure, `paused` while the shared time isn't following the newest, `asking…` while a command waits for your yes, and `not run` after every no. The `ago` moves on each second only while a run is due or a stream is open, so there's no timer at rest. The interaction reviewer found `paused` for commands waiting on your answer or refused, and a refused file panel still saying `waiting for your yes`.
1595. **For veto:** trust questions (`Run "./errors.sh" every 30s for health.fig? (y/n)`) come one at a time and never over a prompt you're typing in: they wait until that command ends. `Esc` is a no. A no shows `not run: you said no` on every panel of that command, and on a lone chart's error figure.
1596. **For veto:** `R` runs every `run` source again and restarts every ended stream, the focused panel's or not. Contract-p4 §5 had `R` re-run every run source or the focused panel's; the latter can't be done, since the dashboard's model keeps no map from panel to source.
1597. **For veto:** a figure's commands stop when no window shows it, and start again, asking nothing already answered, when one does.
1598. **For veto:** the wheel on a dashboard scrolls the page only while the rows overflow and the burst's first event goes up or down without ⌘. Otherwise the burst is the panel's under the pointer: a linked chart's pans or zooms the shared time, ⌘ over a linked tile zooms the shared time about its middle, and a plain wheel over a tile when the rows fit does nothing.
1599. **For veto:** `Tab` follows the panels as laid out on screen, after a row wraps, then the detail panel's stop, and round. In full size `Tab` shows the next panel full size in the same order, where the interaction reviewer found `Tab` and `S-Tab` jumping out of it.
1600. **For veto:** a zoom, pan, brush or key on a linked chart eases the shared time for every linked chart together, and each chart's y refits each frame. A brush held on a following live dashboard holds still as rows come: the slide waits for the release, and applies only if the release left the view following. The interaction reviewer found the brush sliding under the hand, and its release zooming to another time.
1601. **For veto:** one undo history for each dashboard's window, holding the shared time, each panel's view, full size and following; cursors, references, the focus and the scroll aren't undone.
1602. **For veto:** `⏎` on a dashboard opens the focused panel's detail panel at the window's right, and the dashboard lays out in what's left. It follows the panel's cursor, a series row opens the series' tree, and a jump puts the panel's cursor there. On a tile `⏎` opens `figure::detail`, the tile as drawn over the visible time, and follows it as the data changes. The interaction reviewer found focused tiles and graph panels refusing figure keys with the wrong words, and `figure::detail` never used.
1603. **For veto:** `/` on a dashboard finds in the focused panel: the cursor goes to the best match, and `n` `N` go on. A find, once taken, keeps its mark forward until `Esc`, where the interaction reviewer found it not brought forward at all. `/` doesn't light the matches as you type, as it does on a figure alone.
1604. **For veto:** `s` turns the focused chart's settings and builds that panel again in the dashboard's job, not as an undo step. On a graph panel it offers labels, layout and relayout: labels and layout rebuild through the dashboard's job, not as undo steps, and relayout is one dashboard undo step. On a tile, `/`, `t` and `s` say `this panel is a stat` (or a meter, or a sparkline), and every key that doesn't apply says so for the panel's kind.
1605. **For veto:** a chart alone waiting for its command's first output shows a quiet muted line at the top-left margin, `waiting for ./ticker.sh`, not the error figure; a dashboard's waiting panel shows its frame and title (1565).
1606. **For veto:** a chart alone keeps its cursor on its sample by series and x as rows drop from the front only when its data comes from a command; a dashboard's panels always do.
1607. **For veto:** the status line on a dashboard says `health.fig  6 panels`, or `panel 2/6` once one has the focus (m4's mode line), then `following` while a live dashboard follows, then the commands' notes (`updating…`, `stream ended (exit 0)`).
1608. **For veto:** a dashboard's view (its time, the focus, full size, the scroll, following, and each panel's view and cursor) is parked across `\v` and back and kept by the session, your yeses with it; a time that was following comes back following, at the newest. A second window on a dashboard copies the last window's kept view, where the interaction reviewer found it opening at the spec's range.
1609. **For veto:** a panel that comes to draw, as its data or a yes arrives, keeps the view and cursor it was given (a restart's) where they fit, and the first data sets the shared time to `time.range` ending at the newest.
1610. **For veto:** a hovered tile's card is kept inside the window's area, as a chart's is; a lone stat's card ran off the right edge.
1611. **For veto:** a legend key under the pointer brings its series forward in a dashboard's panel even with no cursor there.
1612. **For veto:** `E` and `C` on a dashboard export its base, with no hover and no cursors, laid out at 2× and 1× on the main thread and drawn in the job.
1613. **For veto:** `T`'s menu lists `15m: Last 15 minutes` to `all: All time`, then `custom…`, which asks `Custom range (10:00-11:30, or -2h): `, the times on the newest sample's day. Either can be typed at the first question, and a length (`2h`, `-90m`) or a pair of times typed there wins over the preset the prompt fuzzy-matches, while part of a preset's name, like `24`, still picks it; the interaction reviewer found `2h` giving 24 hours and `5m` 15 minutes. A range ending at the newest follows it.
1614. **For veto:** `Esc` on a dashboard clears the first there is of a gesture under way, the search's find, full size and the reference.
1615. **For veto:** any key the focused panel takes clears another panel's hover and card until the pointer moves, and a size change drops every panel's hover, as contract-p2b §4.4 has a chart do. The interaction reviewer found two cards up at once, and a resize keeping the dashboard's hover and card.
1616. **For veto:** a failed panel's error figure answers what it would alone. A click on it, or on its log's header, turns it over on a release within the click's slop; the log's keys and the wheel work; an entry is marked on hover or press; and `⏎` or a double click goes to its place through `fig-goto-error` with that panel's errors. A panel kept with the stale badge shows its error as a log over it on `!` or a click on the badge. The interaction reviewer found the failed panel ignoring the clicks and keys, and `!` on a badged panel saying `no errors or warnings`.
1617. No colour ramp is new in the surface: the heatmap's are the tiles' (1541, 1542), and the dashboard uses the theme's colours only.
1618. Deviations in the surface. In P4-dash's files: `PanelState::Graph` holds an `Arc<GraphModel>`, since `GraphAct` needs one to ease pins; the `Figure::Sparkline` arms came at the merge (a sparkline is linked when its x is the time field); a chart panel with only a legend key's emphasis is no longer quiet, so a legend hover draws; a hovered tile's overlay goes through `figure::overlay_in` with the window's area; `PanelFigure` carries `#[allow(clippy::large_enum_variant)]`, since `ChartLayout` grew with the heatmap's ramp and the editor moves it to lend it; and tests/dashboard.rs's ops.fig meter row now expects the tiles' natural meter height, 64 px rather than 100, and the next row at 172 rather than 208. source.rs builds dashboards and lone tiles as figures (`Built` with no model) rather than saying they arrive in a later build, `kind_plural` gone, and `build()` passes the live tables. `surface::Keys` gains `find`, `back` and `put` with no-op defaults, lib.rs `pub(crate) view_value_of` and `view_of_value`, and `Fig` a shared `live::Trust`, parking a dashboard's view with its figure's. Its renders came through the editor, from an ignored test, `renders_for_the_look`, writing the harness's base and overlay buffers when `DIE_FIG_RENDERS` is set; live.png at the spec's 15 minutes shows a few seconds of stream as a sliver at the right, so live-zoomed.png adds the same after `T -6s`.
1619. Not built on a dashboard: `s` on a panel as an undo step, but for a graph's relayout (1604); a graph panel's hovered node coming into focus after its dwell, since the dashboard's overlay draws a graph's hover and card only; `/` lighting the matches as you type (1603); `R` for the focused panel's commands alone (1596); and dashboards linked across windows. using-die.md's What isn't there yet has them.

**die-fig phase 4: the review (2026-11-22)**

1620. Three reviewers, each with probes of its own: the look, from renders of every fixture; correctness; and the interaction, against mouse-ux. 34 findings, 33 distinct since one was found twice: 2 blockers, 18 majors and 13 minors. Through your config it opens health.fig, and every panel is presented once its jobs land: each tile's trend and newest dot, and each chart's lines in their series' colours, with nothing drawn at rest. A hover on the left chart at 10:05 presents the overlay alone, with its card, the dashed moment and the area's dot in the right chart, and each tile's mini crosshair on the point the base drew for 10:05's part. A click focuses the chart with its cursor on 10:05 and its frame ringed; `Tab` goes to the right chart; `f` shows it full size and back in its place; `T` goes to the last hour with its chip; a brush zooms both charts as one undo step, and `u` puts the hour back. broken-panel.fig shows its missing file and its mismatch beside four panels drawn, and `!` turns the first over to its log and back with the others untouched. live.fig asks before each command; ticker.sh's rows stream in with the time following the newest; `T` goes to a second; the wheel pans back to paused while rows still come; and `G` follows again. `\v` goes to the text with the figure gone, every process in its commands' groups reaped and none of the test's own left.

1624. How it checks. "Every panel presented after its jobs" is checked after the jobs land: the harness can't hold jobs back, so the moment of frames before the data isn't asserted, but the base's presents cover every frame and each panel's figure is in the pixels presented. A tile's mini crosshair is checked against the trend's points in the panel's own scene, not by colour, since anti-aliased grey text over the dark ground lands exactly on #5a5a5a, the trend line's colour. The live pan comes after `T -1s`, since three seconds of stream can't pan in a 15-minute window clamped to the data; it's the wheel over the stream chart, and `G` is pressed with no panel focused (1592). "The figure closed" is `\v` from live.fig, which removes the figure's buffer and so stops its commands (1597); processes are checked with `ps` by process group, and the test process has no children or zombies left.

**Installing on another Mac: the pinned Rust, a PTY test and lint offline (2026-11-23)**

Feedback from an install on another Mac: rustup couldn't fetch 1.96.0 on a network that blocks static.rust-lang.org. With rust-toolchain.toml and `rust-version` edited to 1.95 by hand, `cargo xtask app` built and DIE ran. One PTY descriptor test failed, and lint stopped on a crate missing from cargo's offline cache.

1650. **For veto:** the pin stays at 1.96.0, and `rust-version` drops to 1.95, the oldest Rust DIE builds with. The pin is for the checks: rustfmt and so budget's counts, clippy, and ci --linux's image, which is unchanged. A build works with any Rust from `rust-version` up. Moving the pin to 1.95 for one machine would have moved the image, the counts and your own Rust. Nothing in DIE needed 1.96. `cargo +1.95.0 check --workspace --all-targets` is clean, and `cargo xtask app` under 1.95.0 made a DIE.app whose binary has only 1.95.0's rustc hash. The workspace's tests under 1.95.0 failed only where the pin's do (keys.rs inside a DIE terminal), plus xtask's budget unit tests, which need a rustfmt that a minimal 1.95.0 hasn't.
1651. **For veto:** where rustup can't fetch the pin, using-die.md offers two ways. One is a mirror (`RUSTUP_DIST_SERVER`, `RUSTUP_UPDATE_ROOT`) or a proxy (`HTTPS_PROXY`). The other is `rustup override set 1.95.0` in the checkout. A directory's override beats rust-toolchain.toml, and `cargo +X` and `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN` beat both. So `cargo xtask app` and `:rebuild` build with it, no tracked file changes, and rustup fetches none of the toml's components or targets. That was tried with rustup 1.29.3 against a dead dist server. No real mirror or proxy was tried: there's neither here. For that Mac: `git restore rust-toolchain.toml Cargo.toml`, pull, then `rustup override set 1.95.0` there.
1652. using-die.md has a new "What a build needs": the Command Line Tools; Rust through rustup; the crates and libghostty-rs; the terminal's engine, already in the checkout; and the fonts, which `cargo xtask app` fetches and checks. The engine is built for Apple silicon only (vendor has only aarch64-apple-darwin), so an Intel Mac can't link DIE, and the docs say so.
1653. **For veto:** `cargo xtask msrv` checks the workspace, every target, with `rustup run 1.95.0`, and every `ci` runs it, not only a change to the build setup. The reviewer showed that clippy's `incompatible_msrv` finds a newer std function or type but not a newer trait impl or macro, such as 1.96's `From<T>` for `LazyLock` or `assert_matches!`. Without msrv in every ci, 1.96-only code would merge green. The cost: ci on a machine without 1.95.0 stops at msrv, naming `rustup toolchain install 1.95.0 --profile minimal`. It checks the Mac only, so code under `cfg(target_os = "linux")` isn't built with 1.95. Rust 1.95.0, minimal, is now in rustup on this Mac, a download I made. A unit test holds the pin no older than `rust-version`.
1654. The PTY test. The feedback doesn't say which failed. die-core's `no_fd_of_one_terminal_reaches_anothers_program` fails here two ways, both reproduced. With `CLICOLOR=1`, common in a Mac's dotfiles, BSD ls colours names on a terminal, so no line's fd parsed. With fds that whatever runs the tests left open without close-on-exec (`exec 7</dev/null 12</dev/null`, as a shell or an agent's harness can), and ls's name order put 12 between 1 and 2. die's pty_fds failed with CLICOLOR only, since its trampoline marks what DIE was started with close-on-exec. Both tests now run `/bin/ls -lL` by path, without CLICOLOR and CLICOLOR_FORCE, and sort the fds by number. All eight combinations pass. Three planted leaks each still fail them: the test's /dev/null without close-on-exec, the PTY opened without it, and `close_on_exec_inherited` skipped.
1655. **For veto:** die-core's test allows the fds its binary was started with, by number, and names them when it fails. A DIE leak can't land on one of those numbers while they're open. die's keeps the strict check, since its trampoline is the product's.
1656. `-L` because on Linux each /dev/fd entry is a link, and `-l` ends its line with the target, so die-core's test, which ci --linux runs, would have found no fds. It came on 2026-11-09, after the last ci --linux (36d60af, 2026-11-07), so it has never run there. Checked with GNU ls on links, not in the container.
1657. The four suites of die-core, die-vim, die and die-term were run under a hostile environment: CLICOLOR and CLICOLOR_FORCE, inherited fds 7 and 12, and a global gitconfig with `commit.gpgsign=true` and `init.defaultBranch=trunk`. That found more, each fixed in its test:
    - die-core's `nothing_is_left_running_after_shutdown` took its job's pid from pgrep, which found none under load ("its job: ParseIntError"). It takes the shell's `$!` now.
    - die-core's `a_save_keeps_extended_attributes_and_acls` runs /bin/chmod, /usr/bin/xattr and /bin/ls: GNU's, first on a PATH, skipped it or failed it (`ls -le: invalid option`).
    - die's `commands_get_the_path_a_login_shell_has_with_cargo_and_local_bin` runs its new login.sh once first. macOS checks a new program the first time it runs (0.13 to 5.2 s here), and the child's command has half a second. 10 of 100 runs failed before, none of 100 after.
    - xtask's faster_checks repositories run none of your git hooks (`core.hooksPath=/dev/null`). A global failing pre-commit hook failed 7 of 30.
    - mutate's property-test runs have cargo's colour off. A CLICOLOR_FORCE coloured cargo's `Finished` line even into the log, so no build time was read, and each mutant's limit was five times the whole first run.
1658. Lint. xtask's checks run cargo `--offline`, and a build of DIE fetches only the crates it builds, so lint's `cargo tree --target all` found no linux-raw-sys on a machine that had only built DIE. Now lint, sources, guards, clean-build, cross-check, mutate and msrv first run `cargo fetch --locked --offline`, and ci does once for its clone. If cargo lacks only crates to download, which it says once `--locked` resolution has matched Cargo.lock, one line says so, and `cargo fetch --locked` fetches Cargo.lock's crates for every target. They come from crates.io, and libghostty-rs at its pinned rev, by ci --linux's check of the lockfile's sources. A missing index entry or git checkout stops with no fetch and says why, since it may be a Cargo.toml dependency the lockfile lacks. On a fresh cargo home each task fetched once, then ran silent with the network blocked. On this Mac's cache lint prints exactly what main's does, and the probe costs about 0.14 s.
1659. **For veto:** ci --linux is unchanged, so on a new machine its host check stops until a task above has fetched once. The fetch line says "from crates.io" even where your `~/.cargo` config sends crates.io to a mirror; the crates are still checked against Cargo.lock's checksums. And the fetch runs on the task's name before its arguments are checked, so a mistyped `cargo xtask lint extra` on a new machine fetches first.
1660. Found, not fixed:
    - die-term's `a_quit_leaves_nothing_running_and_the_session_no_terminal` failed once in a full run (`terminal.rs:141:20: ParseIntError { kind: Empty }`), and passed alone. It's likely `wait_for("job-")` matching the echoed `echo job-$!` before its output: the race fixed in die-core's shutdown test (1657). It may be the PTY failure in your feedback.
    - die-core's `a_flood_doesnt_hold_up_a_key_or_the_frame` is load-sensitive. At a load of 24 to 39 it failed 9 of 17 runs, main's binary as often.
    - `cargo xtask mutate` stops after the unmutated run, on main too: M1's and M3's text no longer occurs in src/text.rs, which changed on 2026-11-17 (dc90107), while mutants.toml last changed on 2026-11-07.
    - cargo sets `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN` for what it starts, so a DIE started under cargo (`cargo run -p die-dev`) hands it to `:rebuild`, over the override and the pin. DIE.app from the Dock has none.
    - die-core's process.rs:541 unit test, and tests in die-markdown and die-orchid-client, run freshly written scripts under short waits, so another Mac's first-run check may hit them as it did die's shell test (1657).
1661. **An accident on this Mac.** A fix agent ran the 1.96.0 cargo by path, without `RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN`, so the build scripts' rustc went through rustup to your default `stable`. Several processes then began updating it to 1.100.0 at once, raced and rolled back. `stable` is still 1.96.0 with every file, but its `lib/rustlib/components` lost the line `rust-std-x86_64-apple-darwin`, so `rustup update stable` may fail with "detected conflict". I wasn't allowed to change `~/.rustup`. The repair is `printf 'rust-std-x86_64-apple-darwin\n' >> ~/.rustup/toolchains/stable-aarch64-apple-darwin/lib/rustlib/components`. DIE's 1.96.0 wasn't touched. About 157 MB of 1.100.0's downloads are left in `~/.rustup/downloads`.
1662. The gate, the lead's, on fix/install-toolchain at 6fc39c6 (ci ran on f9276a5, the same tree): the three branches merged, and one fix of mine, msrv fetching first as the other offline tasks do. `cargo xtask ci` passed fmt, lint (16 crates), budget, clippy, sources (425 files), ghostty, cross-check, msrv ("rustc 1.95.0", 99 s cold), guards and clean-build. test stopped at die-render's tests/keys.rs, the known three inside a DIE terminal. Then `DIE_UNSEEN=1 DIE_PROP_CASES=100 cargo test --workspace --locked --offline --no-fail-fast` passed every target but two. One is keys.rs again. The other is xtask's tests/app.rs: the restart loop's 1,000 ms limit, at 1,132 ms, whose panic poisons the lock, so two more of its tests fail. Unchanged main fails it the same way at this load (1,131 and 1,103 ms), with your Firefox and Moonlight busy and a load of 10 to 13. Earlier, at a lower load, it passed alone.

Not run: `ci --full`'s ignored tests, `ci --linux` (colima), `mutate` (it stops on main too, 1660), S1's on-screen checks, and anything on a real restricted network.

**Gantt charts: a new figure in die-fig (2026-11-25)**

You asked for the `.fig` format to hold gantt charts and for die-fig to draw them: "imagine up a fitting schema that fits with the general fig structure". It's built, on `fig/gantt`: `"type": "gantt"`. Renders are in `die-fig-previews/7-gantt/`, the skill has `gantt.md`, and using-die.md's fig section has a paragraph, **A gantt**. die-fig is at 50,562 lines, from 44,825.

1663. **For veto:** a gantt is a figure of its own, as a graph is, and not a mark of the chart grammar. Rows of a fixed height that scroll, group headings, arrows, milestones and progress don't fit a model of series of samples. What it shares with every figure: the `type` tag, `title` and `subtitle`, `data` and `transform` as a chart's, channels that name fields, `fig` blocks on rows, `tooltip`, `detail` and `view`, and unknown keys tolerated with the warning for a misspelt one.
1664. **For veto:** the tasks are `tasks`, a list in the spec as a graph's `nodes` are, or `data`, rows from a file, a command, the buffer or a dashboard's named data. One of the two. Both read to the same table, so a plan in a CSV and one written inline mean the same.
1665. **For veto:** a task's keys are `id`, `label`, `start`, `end`, `duration`, `after`, `group`, `parent`, `progress`, `milestone` and `fig`; anything else is its data, for the detail panel and the card. In a table they're found by name (`label`, `name`, `task` or `title`; `end` or `finish`; `after`, `depends` or `depends_on`; `group` or `section`; `parent` or `parent_id`). Other names take a channel at the top, `"start": "begins"`, as a chart's `x` names a field.
1666. **For veto:** `after` schedules. A task with `after` and no `start` starts when the last of them ends, so changing one duration moves what follows. A `start` that's given stands, and a task that starts before one it comes after ends is in conflict: its arrow is in the warning colour and its card says by how much. One date alone is a milestone. A task with children and no dates spans them. `duration` is `"3d"`, `"2w"`, `"36h"`, `"90m"`, or a number of days.
1667. **For veto:** in a plan where no date has a time of day and every duration is whole days, an `end` takes in its day: 2 Nov to 6 Nov is five days. One time of day anywhere makes every end the instant it says. This is read off the whole plan; no key sets it.
1668. **For veto:** `"calendar": "weekdays"` makes a duration count Monday to Friday, and a start the model works out never lands on a day off; `{"days": …, "off": […]}` lists more days off. Dates you write stand. I added it after the first example put tasks on Saturdays. A plan with times of day doesn't use it, and warns. There are no working hours.
1669. **For veto:** `today` is on unless the spec says `false`: a line at the clock's now, read when the figure is built, so a figure left open overnight shows yesterday's line until its source changes. A time pins it. `marks` are lines (`at`) and shaded spans (`start` and `end`), each with a `label` and a `fig` block.
1670. **For veto:** bars are coloured by `group`, or by a `color` field. A legend is drawn only when its names aren't the group headings already.
1671. **For veto:** the look. The time axis is along the top and stays put while the rows scroll. Labels are in a gutter at the left, as wide as the widest, at most 38 % of the width. Rows are 28 px and never stretch, so a short plan leaves the window's foot empty. Bars are 14 px; where the plan tracks progress, the part done is solid over the rest at 35 %. A milestone is a diamond with its date beside it. A group's heading has a line across the group's span, and a task with parts is a thin neutral bar with a tick at each end. Weekends and days off are shaded once a day is 6 px wide. `Today` and the marks' labels have a strip of their own under the tick labels, so they never sit on a bar.
1672. **For veto:** arrows are right-angled hairlines. One to a task that starts as the other ends, or before, turns once and comes into the bar from above. I changed that after the first renders, where each such arrow looped round the row and a chain of sixty tasks was a tangle. The bars of tasks with parts were made quieter in the same pass: they'd been the loudest thing in a plan of epics.
1673. **For veto:** the keys. `j` `k` go to the task below and above, `h` `l` to the task that starts before and after, `]` `[` along the arrows, `w` `b` to the next and previous milestone. `+` `-` `0` and the `z` keys act on the time, and `zj` `zk` scroll the rows. `x` `o` `X` act on the name that colours the cursor's task. There is no reference on a gantt, and `m` says so.
1674. **For veto:** the pointer. Hovering a task shows its card; off a bar, the date under the pointer on the axis. A click puts the cursor on a task. A drag across the plot zooms the time to it. ⌥-drag, a middle drag and a drag on the axis pan. The wheel scrolls the rows when they overflow, and pans the time when they don't; ⌘-wheel zooms the time.
1675. **For veto:** a bare table with a `start` column of times and an `end` or a `duration` column is guessed as a gantt, before the tree rule and the chart rules. A CSV with those column names that you meant as a line chart now needs a spec to say so.
1676. **For veto:** a gantt in a dashboard is a panel with its own time and its own scroll. It doesn't share the dashboard's `time`.
1677. Left out, each for later: working hours for a plan in hours, folding a task with parts or a group, a critical path, baselines, a reference task, a gantt panel on the dashboard's time, and bars dragged to reschedule, since a figure never writes its source.
1678. Calls the two builders made, which I read and kept:
    - A plan in hours fits to an hour at the least; a plan of whole days is padded out to whole days.
    - A task whose `fig` block says `hidden` has no row at all, as a graph's hidden node has none.
    - An `end` alone is a milestone at that time, even with `after`.
    - An id wins over a label as a task's name, and a repeated label gets ` (2)`.
    - `]` on a task with one successor walks on down the chain, where the contract's "round them" would never leave it.
    - A setting turned on a gantt panel isn't an undo step, as a chart panel's isn't. Alone, each is one.
    - `fig-spec` on a guessed gantt names the data's file, where it first wrote the buffer itself.
    - A row's error from a CSV that went through a transform has no line, since the line would be wrong.
1679. One fix outside the gantt: `]` `[` on a dashboard's focused graph said "no cursor yet" after its cursor had moved. The check read the window's pane, not the panel's.
1680. The graph's header and card were opened up for the gantt to share (`header_of`, a `Card` over a `CardArea`), not copied. To check nothing else moved, I drew 42 existing figures (charts, graphs, dashboards, tiles and error figures, at rest and hovered) from main and from the branch: every PNG is the same bytes.
1681. **The power-down.** Your Mac powered down about an hour into this. The session's scratchpad under `/private/tmp` came back empty, and with it the worktree, the first parser and the written contract, none of them committed. I rewrote them from the conversation. From then on the worktree and the briefs were under `.claude/worktrees/_gantt/`, which survives a restart; each piece was committed as it compiled; and one cargo ran at a time, niced, at `-j 8`. I don't know which process it was. That night's crash reports are 26 of a `stash` binary run from `/private/tmp` by Python, which wasn't this session's.
1682. **For veto:** nobody has seen a gantt on screen, in a DIE window or otherwise. It's tested through the harness and the fake pixel sink, and drawn by fig_shot.
1683. The gate, mine, on `fig/gantt` at 8e5477f, one cargo at a time, niced: `cargo fmt --all -- --check`; `cargo clippy -p die-fig -p die-dev --all-targets --locked --offline -- -D warnings`; under `DIE_UNSEEN=1`, `cargo test -p die-fig --locked --offline --no-fail-fast`, 732 passed, 0 failed, 8 ignored (the gantt's own are 6 of its spec, 21 of its model and drawing, 12 in the editor); `cargo test -p die-dev --test fig`, 11 passed; `cargo xtask lint` (16 crates); `cargo xtask budget` (die-fig 50,562); `cargo xtask sources` (438 files). The wiring's builder broke three behaviours on purpose (the wheel's meaning, the hover, the cursor of a task that's gone) and four tests failed, then restored them.

Not verified: a gantt whose data comes from a `run` or `stream` command; one polled from a file on disk; a dashboard's gantt panel through a restart; any zone but UTC in the tests, and days the clocks change. Not run: `cargo xtask ci`, `ci --full`, `ci --linux`, `mutate`, the rest of the workspace's tests, and S1's on-screen checks.

## 2026-11-07: 28 packages on `m0/integration`, and none of them on `main`

**Waiting on you:**
1. **Whether this reaches `main`.** A lander's `gh pr merge` was denied by the auto mode classifier as "Merge Without Review", so nothing in this run has reached `main`. That decision is yours. PR #17 (K10b) is still open and the branch supersedes it.
2. **The hand checks** in "What only you can check" below.
3. **Any call below.** You can overturn any of them at any time. The ones I'd read first: 299 (`die-vim`'s guard 7 limit is now 5,790, against 5,000), 300 (`platform::macos` is no longer a thin wrapper), 360 (keys typed during a restart are dropped), 417 (`/` follows `evil-command-line-map` though your `/` is isearch), and 383 (Cmd-Q refuses unsaved work even with a session to keep it).

### How this run worked, and how that differs

You said: "we need to be faster still - fewer tests and more agents". So:
- Everything lands on the branch `m0/integration`, not `main`.
- A builder lands its own work on a gate of fmt, lint, budget, check, clippy and the touched crates' tests. After a rejected push it re-merges and runs only `cargo check`.
- Reviews run behind the landing, and fixes go forward. Before, a review and `ci` came ahead of each merge.
- `ci --full`, `ci --linux` and `mutate` run only at checkpoints.
- Speed, window and slow tests are `#[ignore]`d by default, so plain `cargo test` doesn't run them.

**Final checks on `main` at 7c530fb, before this run:** `mutate` caught 48 of 48 in 3,117 s, and `ci --linux` passed in 495 s. This closes the run the entry below left waiting.

**A correction to the five log commits from before this run.** T0, K6, K9, K10b, R3, F3 and V4 have their reports on the local branches `m0/t0-faster-checks`, `m0/k6-k9`, `m0/k10b-save`, `m0/r3-f3-window` and `m0/v4-visual`, and those log commits never landed. Each says `ci` passed or that it merged. That didn't happen. On `m0/integration` they had a whole-workspace test run with no failures, at 100 property cases, and no `ci`. Their calls all started at 191, so here they're renumbered, 191 to 295. Their references to one another are corrected below.

### What you can do now, on `m0/integration`

`main` still has none of this. Nobody has yet typed into any of it with real keys.

- **Open a file and edit it.** `die file.txt` opens a borderless window without taking focus. `cargo xtask app` builds `~/Applications/DIE.app`, which keeps one instance and restores your session. No report shows that command run for your real bundle.
- **Vim, held to your Evil by the oracle, except that windows have only harness tests.** Normal, insert, replace and visual state, operators, text objects, registers, `.`, macros, `/ ? n N * #`, `:s`, `H M L`, `gj gk`, the scroll keys, `C-u`, and the `C-w` map with `:sp :vs :new :vne :clo :on`.
- **Files.** `:w :w! :q :q! :wq :e :e! :b :N :noh`. `:w` refuses a file changed on disk, and files that change on disk reload.
- **Find things.** `\f` find-file, descending inside the prompt. `\x` command palette, recent first. `\]` switch-buffer. `:kill-buffer`. `\hk` and `\hc` describe a key and a command.
- **Mouse and trackpad.** Click, drag to select, scroll the window under the pointer.
- **Clipboard.** Yanks reach other apps and their copies reach `"+`, with line and block kinds kept between DIE instances.
- **Quit and restart.** `:q` and Cmd-Q quit, and refuse unsaved file buffers. `restart` in DIE.app hands over to a new instance. Splits, cursors, scroll, unsaved text, registers, macros and histories come back.

**Exit tests:** (b) `cargo xtask restart-loop 20` passed: 21 equal digests, slowest restart 580.9 ms against 1 s, in one run on a loaded machine. (d) holds by count (below), except that V7's row also asks for cases from dogfooding, and there are none.
